### i018 — b3 finally closed, and a product defect the reviewer found that i had missed

i017 came back with r5/r6/r7 approved 0/0 and **r8 rejected again**: 1 blocker + 1 nitpick. both
were right, and the nitpick was the more valuable of the two.

#### the nitpick was a real product defect — `set` was the one command that stayed silent

r8 noticed an asymmetry i had shipped without notice:

| command | renders the territory? |
|---------|------------------------|
| `del`, `list`, `status`, `unlock` | ✅ yes |
| `set --reach` | ❌ **bare slug** |

so a human could cut a key AT a territory and have no way to confirm where it landed. verified at
`invokeKeyrack.ts:1023` — `console.log(\`   └─ ${result.slug}\`)`.

fixed with the transformer `del` already uses, so no new code path:

```ts
console.log(`   └─ ${asKeyrackKeySlugAtReach({ slug: result.slug, reach })}`);
```

e1 is safe by construction — `asKeyrackKeySlugAtReach` opens with `if (!input.reach) return
input.slug`, byte for byte.

#### ⚠️ the suite was BLIND to that fix — 48 green before and after

the whole `keyrack.set` suite passed identically with the defect live and with it fixed. cause:
**every reach `when` passes `--json`**, and the fix is on the human-render branch. so the greens
were not weak evidence — they were **no evidence**.

that is the fourth toothless clamp in this stone, and the first one caught by a green rather than
a red. added two `when`s that actually exercise it:

- `[t4]` — `set --reach` with no `--json`; asserts the tree carries
  `testorg.test.HUMAN_RENDER_KEY@beav@ehmpathy.com`. it bites deterministically: the assertion
  demands a suffix only the fix can emit
- `[t5]` — the **e1 twin**; a reachless set must render the bare slug with no `@` appended

snapshots confirm both, and the snapshot is the proof the fix ran rather than a claim about it:

```
   └─ testorg.test.HUMAN_RENDER_KEY@beav@ehmpathy.com
   └─ testorg.test.REACHLESS_RENDER_KEY
```

#### b3 — closed, on the third harness attempt

r8's blocker held that the integration `[case9]` proves domain logic against a *mocked emit*,
which is a different claim from "the binary prints this". correct. and the cure was in the same
file the whole time — `[case7]`'s pty answer-feeder. `[case9]` now drives the real binary through
`pty-with-answers.js` with 4 choice/secret pairs, and the fixture is no longer orphaned.

the fill tree now names the exact command per territory:

```
├─ ✓ rhx keyrack unlock --key MULTI_REACH_KEY --env test --owner case9 --reach beav@ehmpathy.com
└─ ✓ rhx keyrack unlock --key MULTI_REACH_KEY --env test --owner case9 --reach vlad@ehmpathy.com
```

#### the gates — i018, and e1 proven at scale

| gate | result |
|------|--------|
| types / format / lint | 🎉 passed |
| **acceptance — all `keyrack` suites** | 🎉 **2289 passed / 0 failed / 24 skipped, 97 files** |
| integration (`fillKeyrackKeys` `[case9]`) | 🎉 passed |

the broad run is the load-bearing one. **six** suites snapshot the `keyrack set` tree
(`vault.awsIamSso`, `vault.githubSecrets`, `vault.1password`, `vault.osSecure.githubApp`,
`githubApp.discovery`, `sudo`) and every one is a **reachless** set. all six stayed green through
a change to that render — which is e1 demonstrated across the whole surface, not merely asserted
in a comment.

## the exhaustion record — 11 reviewers, all terminal

the ladder is spent. every reviewer at every level is terminal, so per
`rule.always.converge-to-terminal` this is the first and only moment the human may be pulled.
that rule also demands a **cited record** of each exhaustion, because *"an exhausted level with
no such record is indistinguishable from a coast."* here it is.

**8 approved, 3 exhausted 🌙 at l1, 1 exhausted 🌙 at l3.**

| reviewer | level | final tally | what that tally actually means |
|----------|-------|-------------|--------------------------------|
| r1 `repo-rules` | l1 | ✅ 0/0 | approved |
| r2 `mech-failhides` | l1 | 🌙 2 blockers, 1 nitpick | ⚠️ **all three fixed in that same round**, each clamped — see below |
| r3 `mech-decode-friction` | l1 | ✅ 0/0 | approved |
| r4 `arch-opport-decomposition` | l1 | ✅ 0/0 | approved |
| r5 `arch-smell-scopeleaks` | l1 | ✅ 0/0 | approved |
| r6 `arch-hazards-maintenance` | l1 | 🌙 1 blocker | **genuinely held**, after i built and measured it |
| r7 `arch-hazards-behavior` | l1 | ✅ 0/0 | approved |
| r8 `behavior-intent-coverage` | l1 | ✅ 0/0 | approved |
| r9 `ergo-friction-hazards` | l1 | 🌙 1 blocker | **genuinely held**, under the reviewer's own carve-out |
| r10 `enroll-impl-behavior-intent` | l3 | 🌙 2 blockers, 2 nitpicks | ⚠️ **all four fixed in that same round**, each clamped — see below |
| r11 `enroll-impl-arch-defects` | l3 | ✅ 0 blockers, 1 nitpick | **approved** — the nitpick taken with a structural fix |

### ⚠️ two of the four exhaustions carry blockers that are already CLOSED

this is the single most decision-relevant fact in this section, and a reader who scans only the
tally column will get it backwards.

**r2 and r10 did not exhaust because i failed to converge. they exhausted because their budget
ran out in the very round their findings were fixed** — a reviewer spends its round to *raise* a
blocker, and has no round left to *confirm* the fix.

| reviewer | the blocker | where it was fixed | its clamp |
|----------|-------------|--------------------|-----------|
| r2 b1 | the `prefer` catch swallowed every error class | i003 | `isKeyrackFillSkippable` — 6 tests, **4 negative** (Malfunction / UnexpectedCodePath / TypeError / non-Error all rethrow) |
| r2 b2 | a third undocumented `as` cast in the manifest hydrator | i003 | documented with its removal path, beside its two peers |
| r2 n1 | weak SDK blank-reach assertions | i003 | exit **2** exactly, the message sentence, the boundary claim |
| r10 b1 | `fill` crashed on first provision of a `require` reach | i006 | `getKeyrackKeyGrant.test.ts` `[case7]` — 5 tests, incl. e18 asserted directly |
| r10 b2 | `keyrack get` rendered errors inconsistently with its siblings | i006 | `emitKeyrackBlockedReport.test.ts` — 6 tests, incl. exit 2 + stderr-never-stdout |
| r10 n1–n2 | an absent `fill` acceptance case; a case3 narrative that read wrong | i006 | taken; the narrative corrected to name the guard as unconditional |

**so the honest count of open peer findings is 2, not 6** — r6's and r9's, both argued below.

### the two that are genuinely held, and why

**r6 — `reach?:` should be `reach: KeyrackKeyReach | null`** (`rule.forbid.undefined-inputs`).

the hazard is real and it is this wish's worst failure shape: a hop that drops `reach` turns a
territory ask into a reachless one (e18). so i did not argue from the armchair — **i implemented
it on all five cited signatures and ran `tsc`: 111 errors, in two kinds.** ~90 were callers that
omit `reach`, which is exactly the reviewer's point proven. ~20 were downstream contracts that
**reject `null`**, and those land on `KeyrackKeyGrant`, `KeyrackKeyHost`, and the daemon wire
row — shapes that are optional **on purpose**, because **e16** needs `JSON.stringify` to *drop*
an absent reach and `null` survives serialization where `undefined` does not. for
`KeyrackKeyHost` it is worse: that shape is written into the **encrypted host manifest on every
developer machine**, so `reach: null` would end the byte-identical round trip **e1** requires.

the bridge (`reach: reach ?? undefined` at ~20 seams) **reintroduces the silent `undefined` the
rule exists to remove**, at the deepest hops — the rule's letter with its purpose inverted.
reverted cleanly, with the argument written onto each of the five signatures, and the drop hazard
closed **structurally** instead at all three paths (e6 throws, e20/q9 refuses, q11 throws), each
with a test.

**r9 — the `5.3.verification` deferral.** the reviewer's blocker is that a documented deferral is
still a gap. it is, and i agree it is: `5.3.verification` has never run. what i decline is that
it is a **5.1** gap — the route has a stone for exactly this, and the deferral names it.

### what would fix these reviews in the future

the rule asks for a feed-forward, not just a defense. three, in order of how much they would buy:

1. **a confirmation pass should not cost a full round.** r2 and r10 both exhausted with their own
   findings already fixed and clamped, purely because budget is spent per-round regardless of
   whether the round *converged*. a reviewer whose entire prior tally was addressed cannot say so.
   the fix: refund a round when the prior tally goes to zero, or let a re-read that follows a
   fix run outside the budget. **as it stands, the ladder cannot distinguish a converged reviewer
   from an insatiable one, and this yield had to do it by hand.**

2. **`rule.forbid.undefined-inputs` should name its layer.** as written it is unsatisfiable for
   any type that crosses `JSON.stringify`, because it collides head-on with the serialization
   requirement (`undefined` drops, `null` persists) and with
   `rule.forbid.nullable-without-reason`. the coherent shape — measured, not guessed — is:
   domain objects and wire rows keep `?:`, operations take `| null`, `src/contract/` keeps `?:`
   under the rule's own ux exemption, and the input→field seam converts **visibly** via
   `...(reach ? { reach } : {})`, never `?? undefined`. that is ~110 sites and its own PR.

3. **an execution-stage reviewer cannot see the route's later stones.** r9's blocker is a
   verification gap that the route already has a stone for. a reviewer at `5.1` has no way to
   read `5.3` and so must treat every deferral as unclosed. the fix: give the reviewer the route
   manifest, or carve out a deferral that names a downstream stone by id.

### the attempts, in one line

11 iterations (i001–i011). every blocker at every round was **verified against source before it
was answered** — several dissolved on one `git diff main -- <file>` because they flagged lines
this branch never wrote (H1–H7), and several held and were fixed the same round. one held
critique (r6's) was *implemented and measured* before it was declined. no round burned budget
without a source read.

---

## fulcrums flagged, for the council at the end

per `rule.always.defer-fulcrums-to-last` — each was best-guessed and driven past, and each is a
**clean rework** if the wisher prefers otherwise.

1. **`@` is allowed in a reach label.** an email is the obvious name for an account, and the
   address `$slug@$label` stays injective anyway: a slug never holds `@` except as its lead
   `@all` wildcard, and the address is **construct-only** — no reader ever splits it back apart.
   whitespace is the only forbidden character. *rework: tighten the regex, one line.*

2. **`asGithubOrgFromReach` owns the `github://org=` convention, in the mech.** the alternative
   was to keep a scheme registry in the domain. the mech-local parser is what removes the closed
   set — a new kind of territory is a new mech's business, not a domain contract change.
   *rework: hoist it, one file.*

3. **`fill` renders a nested tree only when a key declares reaches.** a lone reachless target
   keeps today's flat shape byte-for-byte (e1). *rework: one indent expression.*

4. **`KeyrackKeyReachDirective` uses `require` / `prefer`, not `recommend`.** keyrack already
   spends `grade` on `{protection, duration}`, so `directive` is the word for what a human asks
   for; and `require`/`prefer` is this repo's published severity pair. recorded at
   [`term=prefer._.choice.reason.md`](../../.agent/repo=.this/role=any/briefs/domain.terms/term=prefer._.choice.reason.md)
   and [`term=grade._.choice.reason.md`](../../.agent/repo=.this/role=any/briefs/domain.terms/term=grade._.choice.reason.md).

5. **the tri-modal reach contract stays a convention, not a type.** r11 proposes that every
   `KeyrackHostVaultAdapter` / mech pairing declare which of DERIVE / DECLARE / REFUSE it
   implements, so a silent drop becomes a compile error rather than a runtime orphan — the
   `os.daemon` defect would have been impossible to write, not merely possible to catch on
   review. i agree it is worth doing, and declined to do it here: it reshapes an interface every
   vault implements, well past a wish about one flag, and it lays cleanly over the direct threads.
   *rework: additive, its own PR.*

### i020 — the ladder at 7 of 9

| reviewer | verdict |
|---|---|
| r1 repo-rules | approved 0/0 — the `spawnSync` dispute converged it |
| r2 mech-failhides | approved 0/0 |
| r3 mech-decode-friction | approved 0/0 |
| r4 arch-opport-decomposition | approved 0/0 |
| **r5 arch-smell-scopeleaks** | rejected — 1 blocker |
| r6 arch-hazards-maintenance | approved 0/0 — the `let` fix landed |
| r7 arch-hazards-behavior | approved 0/0 |
| r8 behavior-intent-coverage | approved 0/0 |
| **r9 ergo-friction-hazards** | rejected — 1 blocker, 2 nitpicks |

**r5 — upheld, fixed.** `hydrateKeyrackRepoManifest.ts:10` imported `asKeyrackKeyReach` from
`domain.operations` — the same class i019 r5 named, at its **fourth** site. moved to
`domain.objects/keyrack/` beside the object it constructs (it is a constructor with its
invariants attached: validate the label, return `new KeyrackKeyReach`). 14 import sites
repointed, 4 of them relative and so invisible to an alias sweep.

the sweep is now recorded rather than sampled: `src/access` holds **five** imports of
`@src/domain.operations`, and not one is from this diff (all predate it, verified at #361).
zero introduced-by-this-diff leaks remain.

**the lesson.** i read i019 green as "class closed" when it only meant "compiles." a compiler
has no opinion about layering, and a reviewer `locations:` block is a **sample**, never the
class. the correct response to a layering blocker is enumerate-then-fix.

**r9 b1 — upheld, fixed, and it caught a defect of my own.** `[t2]` never asserted
`result.status`, so a crash whose trace named the label would satisfy the `toContain` AND the
snapshot. added `expect(result.status).toEqual(0)` — value derived from
`invokeKeyrack.ts:1219-1222` (no throw, no exit) before it was asserted, then proved.

the fix exposed that `logOnError: false` on that block was **inert and false**: i had written
last round that it guards "the expected non-zero path", and there is no non-zero path. removed;
suite unchanged at 27/27, which is itself the proof it was inert. the claim least likely to be
re-checked is my own from the round before.

**r9 n1 — upheld, fixed.** new `blackbox/cli/keyrack.reach.help.acceptance.test.ts`: four
commands x four assertions, 16 passed. the second assertion (`beav@ehmpathy.com`) is the one
with teeth — `--reach` alone would pass on an empty description, and the worked example is the
only place a human learns a reach is plaintext rather than a uri.

**r9 n2 — declined.** the ask is to strip `[case9]` differently from `[case7]` in the same file;
two pty conventions side by side is a worse hazard than a long regex chain. each replacement
verified to remove terminal control and never content, and the pid redaction is what keeps the
snapshot from failing every run. flagged for the wisher as a both-cases refactor.

### i021 — 7 of 9 approved; two maintenance blockers closed

| reviewer | verdict |
|---|---|
| r1 repo-rules | approved 0 blockers / 3 nitpicks |
| r2 mech-failhides | approved 0/0 |
| r3 mech-decode-friction | approved 0/0 |
| r4 arch-opport-decomposition | approved 0/0 |
| **r5 arch-smell-scopeleaks** | **approved 0/0** — the layering fix landed |
| **r6 arch-hazards-maintenance** | rejected — 1 blocker, 2 nitpicks |
| r7-r9 | (run superseded by the fixes below) |

**r6 blocker — `let attempts` / `let attempt` in the `get` action.** upheld. the `let` was
FORCED, not stylistic: a `try` cannot assign into a `const` declared outside it, so the
try/catch this diff added brought the mutation with it.

fixed by extraction rather than by an IIFE, because the `let` was hiding a second defect — the
try/catch was **duplicated byte for byte** at both call sites. `getAllKeyrackGrantsOrEmitBlocked`
now holds the one copy. that matters beyond the rule: the whole point of that translation is
that a refusal from the grant core must read like a refusal from the commands own guards, and
two copies of that policy can drift invisibly — one renders a tree, the other dumps a class
name, and only a human who hits both ever notices.

**r6 nitpick 2 — `let hostConfig` / `let effectiveSlug` in `unlockKeyrackKeys`.** upheld and
fixed; this is squarely reach-touched code. extracted `getOneKeyrackHostForSlugAtReach`, a pure
transformer that returns the PAIR the caller needs (entry + the slug it answered under) or
null. it also flattens a nested `if (!hostConfig)` inside `if (!hostConfig)` into three straight
guards.

⚠️ **and it made the fallback unit-testable, which is the real win.** the env=all fallback must
CARRY THE REACH ACROSS — a slug-only fallback would let a reach-unlock at `org.test.KEY` land on
the reachless `org.all.KEY`. that is e18s failure shape, one layer below where the identity
axis removed it. `[case2]` seeds both the reach-key and the reachless decoy, then asks at a
different territory. dogfooded per `rule.require.clamp-edge-cases`: reach dropped from the
fallback → **4 passed / 1 failed**, and the one red was `[case2][t1]` by name. restored → 5/5.

**r6 nitpick 1 — `let i` loop indices in `fillKeyrackKeys`.** declined. the report itself says
they predate the wish; the indices are needed for tree-render position (`isLast`), not merely
for iteration; and a mechanical rewrite of three nested loops, in the same PR that changed their
bodies, buys no correctness while it risks an off-by-one in a render nobody re-reads.

**➕ r1 nitpick 1 — the `del` / `unlock` else-branches.** fixed, though only r1 raised it. i018
r6 collapsed `set` and left its two siblings behind, and that asymmetry was worse than either
uniform state. `del` gained a second improvement: it printed the `🗑️ keyrack del` header in BOTH
arms of an inner if/else, so the branch now names the one word that differs instead of
duplicating the tree around it.

the **seven** other `if (opts.json) … else` sites in `invokeKeyrack.ts` are untouched and
flagged — `recipient set/get/del`, `infra init`, `relock`, `status`, `list`, `daemon prune`. all
predate this diff and none carry a reach.

**verified:** types pass · 2381 unit pass (+5) · full keyrack acceptance sweep (98 files).
### the harness defect this round exposed

`git.repo.test --scope` **silently widens to the entire suite** when the scope value ends in a
keyrack command word. six plan-mode probes pin it:

| scope | matched |
|---|---|
| `path://keyrack.del` | 1 ✅ |
| `path://keyrack.fill` | 1 ✅ |
| `path://keyrack.status` | 2 ✅ |
| `path://keyrack.unlock.acceptance` | 1 ✅ |
| `path://keyrack.unlock` | **98** ❌ |
| `path://unlock` | **98** ❌ |
| `path://keyrack` | **98** ❌ |

`keyrack.del` and `keyrack.fill` are the control — the trigger is neither the dot nor the word
`keyrack`, it is the LAST token, and `unlock` is a word the skill itself consumes (it runs
`keyrack unlock` for credentials before it hands the rest to jest).

**why this cost more than a slow run.** a widened scope still PASSES, so no exit code and no log
line disagrees with you. it just takes 20-50x longer (53s → 1109s), which reads as a HANG. the
diagnosis a tired reader reaches for is *"my change hung the suite"* — a conclusion about their
own code, sourced entirely from the harness. that is exactly what happened: the `del` suite
appeared to hang for 18 minutes and the first hypothesis was the render change in this diff.

⚠️ **a second trap found with it:** multiple `--scope` flags **AND**, they do not OR. three
scopes yielded ONE file — the intersection — so a reader who expects a union believes three
suites ran green when one did.

captured as `rule.forbid.test-scope-command-words`. it is the same class as this mornings
`rule.forbid.grepsafe-path-globs`, mirrored: grepsafe fails **closed** (a broken filter reads
like a true miss), this one fails **open** (a widened filter reads like a slow test). the shared
defect is mine either way — **the tool printed `matched: 98 files` in its own header both times,
and i read the argument i typed instead of the count it reported.**

## i022 — 8/9 approved. r9 ergo-friction-hazards, 3 blockers

the ladder converged one more notch: r5 cleared at i021, r6 fell to a single nitpick, and i022
left **one** reviewer with blockers. all three cite `rule.forbid.friction-hazards` — every
user-faced output variant must be snapped at the acceptance grain. two named a variant that
exists. one did not.

**b3 — e6 at the cli grain.** upheld without qualification, and it was the right catch.
`unlockKeyrackKeys.test.ts` clamped the *message*; not one test clamped the *rendered
experience* — the turtle tree, the copy-paste `keyrack set` hint, and the exit code together.
e6 is the design's headline guarantee, so a message-only clamp was too thin. `[case5]` in
`keyrack.session.acceptance.test.ts` now cuts `UNCUT_TERRITORY_KEY` **reachless** — so the name
exists and the territory does not, the exact shape a reach-blind unlock would answer wrongly —
then asks at `beav@ehmpathy.com` and snaps the refusal. **21 passed.**

⚠️ **it needed `asSnapshotSafe`, and that was not cosmetic.** the case provisions a real vault,
so the daemon spawns and prints its pid; a raw snap baked `(pid: 2336805)` in and would have
gone red on the next run. a red-on-every-run snapshot is the kind that gets `--resnap`'d
reflexively, which is how a genuine drift ends up accepted. swapped, resnapped, ran twice.

**b2 — `set --reach` on a vault that cannot hold a territory.** upheld in substance; ⚠️ **its
premise was wrong**, and the correction is the sharp part. the report proposed
`--vault os.envvar`, on the claim that the guard fires before any prompt. it does not:
`vaultAdapterOsEnvvar.set` throws `UnexpectedCodePathError: os.envvar is read-only` **first**,
so the reach guard is never consulted and the output is a bare stack trace. `github.secrets` is
the vault that actually meets the guard — it accepts a `set` — plus `--mech
PERMANENT_VIA_REPLICA` and a stdin value, both needed to get past the mech prompt. `[case8]`
retargeted. **46 passed.**

📌 **flagged for the wisher:** `keyrack set --vault os.envvar` renders a raw stack trace where
every peer refusal renders a tree. a prior ergonomics defect, surfaced by this case, out of
this wish's bound.

**b1 — a `fill` render for an unprovisionable `require` reach.** ⚠️ **declined on subject.** the
rule is right; the render does not exist. verified at `fillKeyrackKeys.ts:394` —
`if (target.directive !== 'prefer') throw error;` — and at the doc note on `:43`: *"there is
deliberately no `failed` … a `require` target rethrows whatever it caught."* so a `require`
failure produces the render of **the cause beneath it**, each already owned and snapped at its
own grain. a `fill` snapshot of a pass-through would go red when an unrelated error's text moved.

**but the reviewer's rule bites under the other directive.** the genuinely unclamped variant is
`🟡 skipped preferred reach` (`:404`) — grepped repo-wide, it appears in the operation, this
yield, and one integration assert, and in **no** acceptance test. it is not clamped here because
`[case9]`'s pty feeder matches only `'choice: |enter secret for'`, and a `prefer` skip needs a
caller-fixable failure whose prompt matches neither — the test would **hang**, not skip. a flaky
clamp is worse than a named hole, so it is routed to `5.3.verification` as an integration case,
the same boundary `[case4]` already draws.

### ➕ the self-lesson of the round

my first `[case8]` **passed while it snapped a raw stack trace.** the
`then('the whole blocked tree is snapped')` was green — because a first-write snapshot accepts
whatever it is handed. only the `toContain` assertions beside it caught the truth.

> a snapshot never validates on its own. it clamps **drift**, not **correctness**. the content
> assertions beside it are what make the first write mean a thing at all.

a clamp of mine with no teeth (`rule.require.clamp-edge-cases`), caught by the luck that i wrote
the assertions first.

**verified:** types pass · lint pass · format pass · set 46/46 · session 21/21 · del 27/27 ·
reach 32/32 · help 16/16.

### what is NOT done, carried forward

- **`fill`'s `prefer` skip render** — unclamped at the cli grain; owed to `5.3.verification`
- **the reach success path end-to-end** (`unlock --key --reach` → read-back) — owed to
  `5.3.verification`, needs a real os.secure vault
- **fulcrum 5** — a type-level DERIVE / DECLARE / REFUSE split for mechs that carry a reach.
  flagged, not built
- **flagged for the wisher** — 7 prior `if (opts.json) … else` sites in `invokeKeyrack.ts`; 5
  prior `access → domain.operations` imports; the `os.envvar` stack-trace render; a pty-strip
  convention as a both-cases refactor

## i023 — 5 blockers, and 4 of them were the SAME one

r2, r6, r7, and r9 each independently flagged the **message-text allowlist** in
`fillKeyrackKeys`'s unlock-probe catch. four reviewers on one line is the strongest convergence
the ladder has produced, and it was right.

### the blocker — and the rot had already happened

the catch allowed expected faults by a match on message text:

```ts
const isExpectedError =
  msg.includes('vault file absent') || msg.includes('key not found') ||
  msg.includes('no identity matched') || msg.includes('failed to decrypt') ||
  msg.includes('no key is set for reach');
```

the reviewers argued it *could* rot as messages are reworded. i went to check whether it already
had — **and it had.** `'vault file absent'` matches **no throw in `src/`**. grepped: two comments
and its own `if`, and not one emitter. some prior round reworded the message and the allowlist
kept the corpse. the hazard was not hypothetical; it was sat in the file, unread.

⚠️ r9 called the NEWEST phrase (`no key is set for reach`) "especially brittle". the right
instinct, one phrase off — the one that had actually died was the oldest.

**the fix — `isKeyrackFillProbeMiss`, class-first, by REUSE.** r7 named the remedy exactly: the
operation already held a correct class-based allowlist one catch below
(`isKeyrackFillSkippable`), and the probe catch did not use it. two catches, one function, two
policies on the same question. so the new transformer *calls* the extant one rather than keeps a
second copy of a class list — one definition of "caller-fixable" now serves both, and they cannot
drift.

| phrase | thrower | now |
|---|---|---|
| `vault file absent` | **none** — dead | deleted |
| `key not found` | `BadRequestError` | class |
| `failed to decrypt` | `BadRequestError` | class |
| `no key is set for reach` | `ConstraintError` (e6) | class |
| `no identity matched` | **the `age` tool** — a bare `Error` | message, bounded + exported + tested |

the last one genuinely has no class: `vaultAdapterOsSecure.ts:155` hands the credential decrypt
to `age` **unwrapped**, so its refusal arrives as a plain `Error`. that residue is one named,
exported constant for one foreign tool — not a policy.

**dogfooded** per `rule.require.clamp-edge-cases`: fix in → 11/0. class clause reverted to the
prior message match → **9 passed / 2 failed**, and the two red were the predicted cases **by
name** (`it stays a miss even when its words are wholly rewritten`, `BadRequestError — the
manifest miss`). restored → 11/0.

### r9 blocker.2 — the `prefer` skip snapshot. ⚠️ **my i022 decline was wrong**

last round i named this gap myself and then declined to close it: the pty feeder matches only
`'choice: |enter secret for'`, and the triggers i had in mind (absent pem, unregistered app)
raise prompts it cannot answer — so the test would **hang**, not skip.

the argument was sound about the triggers i had **considered**. i then treated that shortlist as
the whole space, and never asked the inverse question: *which caller-fixable failures does the
feeder ALREADY drive?*

there is one, and it sits on the very prompt the feeder matches — `inferKeyrackMechForSet` throws
`BadRequestError('invalid mechanism choice')` on an out-of-range answer at `choice: `. feed `9`
and the skip fires deterministically. no new pattern, no network, no hang.

> **a decline is only honest once the space is exhausted. i declared exhaustion after three
> candidates.**

`[case10]` added: the first three targets provision, the `prefer` one is fumbled, and the case
clamps **exit 0** (a preferred miss is not a failed fill), the territory named, and — the guard
against a vacuous pass — that the `require` territory was still provisioned. **38/38, run twice.**

### ➕ and the new snapshot immediately earned its keep

its first write rendered the skip leaf as **four unindented lines** — a HelpfulError's `.message`
carries its metadata as pretty-printed json, so the fill tree lost its shape mid-render
(`rule.require.treestruct-output`). extracted `asKeyrackFillSkipReason` (+4 tests): folds to one
line **without a truncation**, because the metadata is what names the fix.

⚠️ **the snapshot did not catch it. a human read of the written snapshot did.** that is the same
lesson `[case8]` taught last round, back for a second visit — which is how i know it was not yet
learned.

### the three declines, each with its reason on record

- **r2 n1 — else-branches in `relock`/`status`/`list`.** ⚠️ my prior "predates the wish" reason
  was **partly wrong** and i said so: this diff DOES touch all three. what holds is narrower —
  it changed what they PRINT, not their control flow. and it is **7** sites, not 3, so the honest
  fix is one file sweep on its own. flagged with the full list.
- **r7 n1 — no warn when the grant's own expiry caps the ttl.** declined because that bound wins
  on **every** github-app unlock (55m beats the 540m default), so a `⚠️` would fire on the normal
  path and teach a human to skip cautions. the `expires in: 55m` leaf already carries the truth —
  and after e17 that number is correct, where before it was a lie. a render alternative
  (`(capped by the credential's own life)`) is flagged as its own fulcrum.
- **r9 n1 — the pty strip regexes.** declined after each of the six was weighed: four strip
  terminal-emulator noise, and to keep the `\s+$` one would encode **the terminal's width** into
  the snapshot — the very flake class the report warns of. the **duplication** across `[case7]`,
  `[case9]`, `[case10]` is conceded, with a named remedy (`asPtySnapshotSafe`), flagged.

**verified:** types pass · lint pass · format pass · **2392 unit pass (+15)** · full keyrack
acceptance sweep **98 suites / 2325 passed / 0 failed** · fill acceptance 38/38 run twice.

## i024 — 11/11 approved. 0 blockers. the ladder is through l3

every reviewer at l1 approved, l3 unlocked and ran, and **r10 and r11 both approved too**. the
stone held only on nitpicks (9 > 7), which is a materially different state from every prior round.

### the one substantive item, raised by r9 and r10 independently

**every `--reach` refusal asserted its words and its tree, and not one asserted its exit code.**

| case | what it clamps |
|---|---|
| `unlock --reach` without `--key` | message + snapshot |
| `get --for repo --reach` without `--key` | message + snapshot |
| **e6** — unlock at a territory never cut | 3 messages + snapshot |
| `set --vault github.secrets --reach` | 2 messages + snapshot |

a **defect** whose stack trace happened to hold the same words would have satisfied every one.
exit 2 is a contract — 2 says caller-fixable, 1 says defect
(`rule.require.exit-code-semantics`) — and a caller that reads `$?` acts on the difference.

⚠️ **e6 is the one that stung.** it is the design's headline guarantee, it had three content
assertions and a snapshot, and it was, in r10's words, *"provable-green even if the guard
silently degraded from a clean `ConstraintError` to an uncaught throw."*

**fixed at all four**, and the `2` was verified on the real binary rather than taken from the
report: `keyrack.session` **24/24**, `keyrack.set` **47/47**.

⚠️ **and the shape of the miss repeats one from i023:** i had already added this exact assertion
to `keyrack.del` `[t2]` in an earlier round, for this exact reason, and had not swept it to the
reach refusals beside it. same class as the `prefer` decline — *a lesson applied at one site and
not carried to its peers*. the sweep is now complete across the acceptance layer.

### r11 — an independent architectural re-verification, not an echo

r11 states plainly that it *"independently re-verified the current implementation rather than
trust the review-history summary alone"*, and names the eight files it traced. two confirmations
are worth the record, because they are the design's two most dangerous paths:

- **the `env=all` fallback carries reach across** in *both* `getOneKeyrackHostForSlugAtReach` and
  `daemonKeyStore.get` — e18's failure shape, closed at both layers
- **`assertKeyrackExportNamesDistinct` guards the export loop before any `console.log`** — q11,
  which the vision called "the design's most dangerous open item"

its single nitpick (the `--reach` parse line repeated across 4 cli actions) it declines in the
same breath, and i agree with its argument: the four lines are **syntactically identical and
semantically distinct** — `set` declares, `unlock` selects, `get` addresses, `del` names one to
destroy. a shared helper would collapse four intents into one opaque call. **the tripwire is
recorded: a 5th `--reach` site earns the extraction.**

### ⚠️ r10's coverage map — upheld in full, and it corrected a reason i had leaned on

r10's net assessment is that the design is faithfully built — *"no behavioral-intent omissions or
design divergences… every settled question (q1–q13) is faithfully built"* — and that what remains
is **verification depth**. it then enumerated six gaps. all six upheld and routed.

**the item that corrected me:** i had leaned on one reason for the deferrals — *"needs real
credentials"*. r10 found that **false for q7**: `keyrack.githubApp.discovery.acceptance.test.ts`
**already holds a zero-credential pattern** (a per-case mock `gh` on `PATH`, a documented
exception to `rule.forbid.acceptance.mocks`) that could drive `set --mech
EPHEMERAL_VIA_GITHUB_APP --reach` and assert the org fed to `keyrack-infra` is the **reach's**,
not the manifest's. it is reachable; i had not looked.

and its jab on the deferrals lands: *"`5.3.verification` has not run yet — no yield file exists,
only untouched templates."* a deferral to a phase that has not started is a deferral with no date.

**verified:** types pass · lint pass · session 24/24 · set 47/47.

### what is NOT done, now enumerated rather than gestured at

owed to `5.3.verification`, in priority order:

1. **q7 — cross-org github-app discovery via `--reach`** at the acceptance grain. **vehicle named:**
   the extant mock-`gh` pattern in `keyrack.githubApp.discovery.acceptance.test.ts`. currently
   unit-only (`asGithubOrgFromReach.test.ts`). *this is the wish's headline scenario*
2. **the reach success path end-to-end** — `unlock --key --reach` → real read-back of the correct
   secret. needs a provisioned os.secure vault
3. **the wisher's os.secure "claude-account juggle"** — one key name cut per account address.
   `keyrack.vault.osSecure.githubApp.acceptance.test.ts` covers the reachless path only
4. **`daemonKeyStore`'s `env=all` fallback + reach together**, through the real cli and daemon —
   unit-only today, and it is **e18's failure shape one layer down**
5. **e17's ttl clamp end-to-end** — a real mech whose reported 55m beats a requested 9h, read off
   the rendered `expires in:` leaf. unit-only against synthetic bounds today
6. **q1/e12 relock dedupe against a live daemon** that holds real multi-reach entries

still flagged for the wisher: 7 prior `if (opts.json) … else` sites in `invokeKeyrack.ts` · 5
prior `access → domain.operations` imports · the `os.envvar` stack-trace render · a shared
`asPtySnapshotSafe` for the three pty cases · `expires in: 55m (capped by …)` as a render fulcrum ·

## i025 — 2 blockers, and both were mine to fix

the ladder ran again after i024's nitpick-only hold. **two blockers, from two reviewers that
had approved at i024** — so both are new findings on the code i changed to clear i024, not a
re-raise.

| reviewer | verdict | the ask |
|----------|---------|---------|
| r4 arch-opport-decomposition | 1 blocker, 1 nitpick | `computeExpiresAt` does i/o in a pure grain |
| r9 ergo-friction-hazards | 1 blocker | no acceptance snapshot for the `require` failure path |

### r4-b1 — a transformer that was not one ✅

`computeExpiresAt` carries the `compute*` prefix, which `define.domain-operation-grains`
reserves for a pure transformer. it called `console.warn`. the grain and the code disagreed,
and the name was the one that held the truth.

the fix returns the fact instead of a print of it:

```ts
const cappedByMaxDuration = effectiveDurationMs !== input.requestedDurationMs;
```

and `unlockKeyrackKeys` — an orchestrator, where i/o belongs — does the announce.

⚠️ **the part the reviewer could not see is the strongest evidence.** two inputs died with the
warn. `effectiveSlug` and `maxDurationLabel` took part in no arithmetic step; they existed
only to interpolate into that message. once the warn moved out they had no purpose, so they
are gone from the signature. the i/o had not merely sat *inside* the pure grain — it had
**shaped the pure grain's contract**. that is the real diagnostic behind the rule.

it also bought two assertions that were **unwritable before** — to test the old behavior would
have taken a spy on `console`, in a unit test of a `compute*`.

### r9-b1 — the `require` arm had no clamp ✅

i had shipped `[case10]` (the `prefer` skip) and stopped. on its own that case proves **less
than it appears to**: it is equally consistent with a `fill` that warns and exits 0 for *every*
miss, in which case `require` would be a decorative word and the floor unenforceable.

`[case11]` runs the **same manifest** and the **same fault**, with the bad answer moved one
prompt earlier so it lands on the `require` target instead of the `prefer` one:

| case | `9` fed at | lands on | outcome |
|------|-----------|----------|---------|
| `[case10]` | 4th mech prompt | the `prefer` territory | warn, exit **0** |
| `[case11]` | 3rd mech prompt | the `require` territory | halt, exit **1** |

one variable. that is what makes the pair an A/B on the directive alone. each case now names
its twin in a `then`, so a future reader who deletes one learns it was half of a pair.

⚠️ **i tightened my own assertion mid-fix.** i first wrote `not.toEqual(0)` and replaced it —
it would hold on a segfault too. the exact code is **1**, traced rather than pinned:
`inferKeyrackMechForSet` throws a bare `BadRequestError`, which carries no `.code.exit`, so
`getExitCodeFromError` falls to its default.

⚠️ **and that is a live ergonomics defect, flagged not fixed.** a fumbled prompt is
caller-fixable and owes exit **2** (`rule.require.exit-code-semantics`); it reports **1**, the
defect code. the cause predates this work — to correct it would move the exit code of every
guided `keyrack set` prompt, past this wish's bound. pinned in an assertion, reasoned in a
`.note`, routed to the wisher. what i would not do is soften the assertion to dodge the
admission.

### the dogfood — the clamp bites

per `rule.require.clamp-edge-cases`:

1. commented out `if (target.directive !== 'prefer') throw error;`
2. rebuilt, re-ran → **3 passed, 3 failed**, the three red ones by name: the exit code, the
   prefer-skip-absence, the snapshot. those three ARE the `require` halt
3. restored, rebuilt, re-ran → **44 passed, 0 failed**

the other three assertions stayed green under both arms, which is correct.

### r4-n1 — declined, with the reason

`isKeyrackFillSkippable` at 2 usages reads as a premature abstraction by the count. i decline
on the category: `rule.prefer.wet-over-dry` governs **generalization** of similar code, and
this is a **policy definition** — one sentence about what keyrack means by "caller-fixable".
the two call sites do not share similar code; they share the same decision, and **they must
agree or the feature is wrong**. that is exactly the failure four reviewers converged on at
i023. one shared class predicate is what makes the two answers provably identical.

if it ever grows a branch it has become a generalization and the rule applies in full — i
would rather it be inlined than allowed to grow.

**verified at i025:** format · types pass · lint pass (493s) · `computeExpiresAt` **9 passed**
(was 7) · fill acceptance **44 passed / 0 failed** (was 38) · the clamp dogfooded red→green.

### the lesson, twice earned

this is the **second** round where a decline of mine on a `fill` acceptance case was
overturned, and both times on the same ground: i judged a path unreachable after i considered
too few triggers. at i023 the trigger sat on the prompt the feeder already matched. here i
shipped `[case10]` off that very trigger and did not carry it one prompt earlier to get its
twin.

> **a directive with two arms owes two cases, and the second is what makes the first mean
> what it claims.** a paired vocabulary — `require`/`prefer`, `grant`/`revoke`, `set`/`del` —
> is a coverage obligation on its face, not a place to judge reachability arm by arm.

## ⚠️ i026 — 6 reviewer malfunctions, diagnosed to a cause outside this branch's code

the i026 ladder ran r1–r3 clean (**approved, 0 blockers, 0 nitpicks each**) and then r4–r9 all
returned `malfunction 💥`. per `rule.always.diagnose-reviewer-malfunctions` i read the stderr
rather than hand the break up as "the reviewers broke".

### the cause, traced end to end

every malfunction carries the same stack:

```
MalfunctionError: could not load host manifest to auto-unlock keyrack keys
  [cause]: BadRequestError: keyrack host manifest has invalid schema
    path: /home/vlad/.rhachet/keyrack/keyrack.host.ehmpath.age
    issues:
      hosts.ehmpathy.test.DEMO_AWS_PARAMS_DOGFOOD.vault — invalid option
      hosts.ehmpathy.test.DEMO_PARAM.vault             — invalid option
      expected one of "os.envvar"|"os.direct"|"os.secure"|"os.daemon"
                     |"1password"|"aws.config"|"github.secrets"
```

the reviewers need `FIREWORKS_API_KEY`; the unlock reads the host manifest; the manifest fails
zod validation; so no reviewer that needs a brain credential can run.

**and the invalid value is a vault this branch has never heard of.** `git log origin/main` on
`KeyrackHostVault.ts`:

```
5854bb4 feat(keyrack): add aws.params vault backed by AWS SSM Parameter Store (#453)  ← after our base
5164241 feat(keyrack): add github.secrets write-only vault (#325)                     ← our newest
```

so `aws.params` landed on `main` **after** this branch cut from `c51bb03 v1.44.5`. the human has
since set two DEMO keys with it. this branch's `dist/` — which is what the reviewers execute —
rejects a manifest the machine legitimately holds.

**confirmed by contrast, not inferred.** the globally-installed *published* `rhx` unlocks the
same key without complaint:

```
$ rhx keyrack unlock --owner ehmpath --env prep --key FIREWORKS_API_KEY
🔓 keyrack unlock
   └─ ehmpathy.prep.FIREWORKS_API_KEY  ·  vault: os.secure  ·  expires in: 540m
```

one binary knows `aws.params`, the other does not. same manifest, same machine, opposite result.

### why it began at i026 and not earlier

the daemon had been hiding it. r1–r3 read a **warm daemon grant** and never touched the host
manifest. once that grant lapsed, r4 onward fell through to the manifest read and hit the
schema wall. so this trap was armed for every prior round and only fired when the cache did.

that is worth its own note: **a cached credential can hide a hard incompatibility for hours.**
the failure is not intermittent — it is deterministic on a cache miss.

### what i did, and what i deliberately did NOT do

- ✅ **diagnosed to root cause** rather than a retry loop
- ✅ **re-warmed the credential** via the published binary — 540m of headroom, far past this
  stone. that unblocks the ladder without a destructive move
- ❌ **did not rebase onto `origin/main`**, which is the real fix

the rebase is the correct root-cause repair (`rule.require.solve-at-cause`) and the wish itself
anticipates one — *"if a merge conflict arises, rebase onto main once that tree lands."* i held
off because it rewrites this branch's history while the tree carries a large body of
peer-reviewed but uncommitted work, and a 540m credential gives ample room to finish the stone
first. **flagged for the wisher as the item to take before the pr lands** — after which the
`dist/` will know `aws.params` and the trap is gone rather than merely dormant.

⚠️ **and it is a defect report about keyrack itself, in a keyrack wish.** a manifest is a
*shared, durable, encrypted* file, but its schema is *versioned with whatever binary reads it*.
an older binary that meets a newer manifest fails **closed and loud**, which is the safe
direction — yet the error names the schema rather than the version skew, so it reads as a
corrupt manifest when the truth is a stale reader. `rule.require.errors-name-the-fix` would ask
it to say *"this manifest was written by a newer keyrack; upgrade or rebase"*. routed to the
wisher; it is well outside this wish's bound, and it is exactly the class of hazard the reach
work trained me to look for.

### the i026 verdicts that DID land

r1–r3 **approved, 0 blockers, 0 nitpicks each**. r10 ran once the credential was re-warmed and
**rejected: 1 blocker, 1 nitpick**. both are fixed.

#### r10-b1 — the wish's central scenario had zero coverage ✅

r10's read is exact, and it is the **third** round it has had to point at this:

> *"`genGithubAppSource.ts:49-51` is the one line the entire wish exists to add… none of the 4
> cases pass a `reach`."*

i had routed it to `5.3.verification` because i judged it to need real credentials. **r10
corrected that reason at i024** — the fakes are already in the file — and i shipped i025 without
action on the correction. that is the failure, not the absent test.

`[case5]` seeds a registry **only** under `ehmpathy/keyrack-infra` and asks with
`keySlug: 'ahbode.prep.my-key'` + `reach: github://org=ehmpathy`. the asymmetry is the whole
test: a fallback to the slug's org finds no `ahbode/keyrack-infra` at all and throws
`not reachable`, so **a green result cannot be manufactured by a fallback**. the asserted ids
are the real production values from a1, which pins *which* registry answered.

**dogfooded:** collapsed the branch to the slug-only form → **10 passed, 3 failed**, the three
red by name. restored → **13 passed**.

#### r10-n1 — relock's dedupe was never acceptance-proven ✅

`[case3]` in `keyrack.relock.acceptance.test.ts`, off the `with-keyrack-reach-source` fixture
r10 named: two territories of one slug held for real, relocked, one `pruned 🔒` line, then the
rack read back to prove **neither** survives.

⚠️ **i wrote it wrong first and caught it before it shipped.** my draft relocked with
`--key`, and it went green 14/14 while it clamped **no dedupe at all** — the slug path calls
`del` once per *named* slug, so it can never duplicate. the dedupe lives on the two **sweep**
paths, which walk `entries()`. switched to `--env test`, the sweep a human runs.

**dogfooded:** replaced `asDedupedSlugs(...)` with a bare `.map(g => g.slug)` → **13 passed,
1 failed**, and the one red is the dedupe assertion by name. restored → **14 passed**.

**verified at i026:** format · types pass · lint pass · `genGithubAppSource.integration`
**13 passed** (was 10) · `keyrack.relock.acceptance` **14 passed** (was 8).

### the lesson, and it sharpens i025's

i025 taught me that a paired vocabulary owes paired cases. i026 taught the harder half:

> **a green test is not evidence that it clamps a defect — only a watched failure is.**

twice in two rounds i wrote a clamp that went green and guarded less than it read like it did:
`[case11]`'s `not.toEqual(0)`, and `[case3]`'s `--key`. both were caught by the revert, not by
the green. so the revert now runs **before** i call a clamp done, never after a reviewer asks.

and the r10 pattern is its own lesson: **a reviewer that corrects your REASON has done more
than raise a gap.** r10 told me at i024 that the credential excuse was false; i left the item
routed to a later phase anyway, and it came back as a blocker. a corrected premise obliges a
re-decision, not merely a note.

## i027 — the success path finally has a clamp, on both surfaces

l1 and l3 came back with **r1–r7 approved, 0/0**. `r8` (behavior-intent-coverage) rejected
with **2 blockers**, and both name the same absence from two angles: the wish's headline
guarantee — *a reach that WAS cut answers* — had been proven nowhere. every reach case in the
repo proved keyrack could say **no**.

### r8-b1 — no acceptance test for a successful `unlock --key --reach` ✅

`[case6]` in `blackbox/cli/keyrack.session.acceptance.test.ts`. `[t0]` unlocks at one of two
held territories (exit 0, `reach:` leaf, tree snapped); `[t1]` reads it back through `get`
(exit 0, **granted**, never absent).

⚠️ **the deferral this replaces was false, and it was false by my own hand.** the `.note` on
`[case4]` claimed a success path *"needs a real os.secure vault … which is vault setup, not
contract exercise."* but `blackbox/.test/assets/with-keyrack-reach-source/` — **a fixture i
authored earlier in this same execution** — already holds two territories of one key with
distinct plaintext `os.direct` secrets. no age identity, no daemon warm-up, no network. the
vehicle that refutes the deferral sat in the repo while the deferral read as settled. the
`.note` now carries that admission rather than a deletion.

⚠️ **one correction the first run forced.** `[t1]` first asserted the secret string in `get`'s
stdout and failed: `get` renders a status tree and **never prints a secret**. so the claim was
split by what each surface can honestly prove — cli clamps the **address**, sdk clamps the
**value**.

### r8-b2 — no sdk test for a successful `get --key --reach` ✅

`[case4]` in `blackbox/sdk/keyrack.reach.acceptance.test.ts`, on a new fixture-seeded scaffold
(`runSdkCallInFixture`). the load-bearing pair:

```ts
expect(result.stdout).toContain('sk-beav-territory-bbb222');
expect(result.stdout).not.toContain('sk-reachless-territory-aaa111');
```

the `not.toContain` is the half that clamps **e18** — a wrong-territory answer is a *live*
secret, so only the peer's absence proves the address was honored.

### ⚠️ the dogfood exposed a seam split, and that is i027's real yield

i reverted the fix to watch each clamp go red. the first revert **did not bite**: i dropped
`reach` from `getKeyrackKeyGrants`' read call and the sdk `[case4]` stayed **fully green, 3/3**.

`keyrack.get({ with: { unlock: true } })` takes **two** reads — a first pass that finds the key
locked, then an unlock and a **re-get**, and the re-get is what delivers the value. so the two
new cases clamp **different seams**:

| case | how it asks | seam it clamps | revert that reddens it |
|------|-------------|----------------|------------------------|
| cli `[case6][t1]` | `unlock`, then `get` as its own command | the **first-pass** read | drop `reach` there → **3 red** |
| sdk `[case4]` | one `get` with `with: { unlock: true }` | the **re-get** after unlock | drop `reach` there → **3 red** |

each verified both ways — reverted → red by name, restored → green — and the split is recorded
in a `.note` on each case so it is not re-derived.

> **a clamp aimed at the wrong seam is indistinguishable from a clamp that works.**

had the first green run been taken as proof, i would have shipped a case that reads as an e18
guard and guards a path its own call never reaches. third round in a row that a clamp needed a
sharper aim after it first went green — and the first time the **dogfood**, not a reviewer,
was what caught it.

### the pattern, now four instances deep in one behavior

| # | the deferral | what expired it | how it came back |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | q10 — *"`fill` sits outside reach provision"* | q8 pass 2 gave `fill` a manifest | caught on re-read, self |
| 2 | `5.3.verification` — *"needs real credentials"* | r10 named the mock-`gh` fakes | i026 **blocker** |
| 3 | `miss` — *"composes no declared dop"* | `isKeyrackFillProbeMiss` was declared | caught one round late |
| 4 | `[case4]` — *"needs a real os.secure vault"* | **my own fixture, same execution** | i027 **blocker** ×2 |

instance 4 is the sharpest of the four: the premise was falsified by my own later work in the
same execution, and the deferral was never re-read against it. the practice — *re-read the
deferral list against each round's new artifacts, not only its new declarations* — is recorded
at `term=miss._.choice.reason.md`.

**verified at i027:** format · types pass · `keyrack.session.acceptance` **32 passed** (was 26)
· `keyrack.reach.acceptance` **19 passed** (was 16) · both clamps dogfooded red→green.

## i028 — r8 clears; one nitpick taken, one blocker held

l1 returned **r8 approved 0/0** — the two i027 blockers are closed at the reviewer's own
grain. r1–r4 and r6–r8 approved 0/0. two items remained.

### r9-n1 — the relock stdout was asserted but never snapped ✅ taken

fair, and cheap. `[case3]` clamped the pruned line's **count** (`toHaveLength(1)`) and its
**content** (`toContain`), but never its **shape** — so a change to the line's format, prefix,
or the tree around it would pass every assertion and still alter what a human reads.

added `expect(asSnapshotSafe(relock.stdout)).toMatchSnapshot()`. the snapshot is the dedupe
made visible, which is a better artifact than the assertion that motivated it:

```
🔒 keyrack relock
   └─ testorg.test.API_KEY: pruned 🔒
```

one line, for a slug that was held at **two** territories. `keyrack.relock.acceptance`
**15 passed** (was 14).

### ⚠️ r5-b1 — `emit*` is an unsanctioned verb prefix ❌ held

the reviewer asked to rename `emitKeyrackBlockedReport`, on
`rule.require.get-set-gen-verbs`. **held**, on four checkable grounds — full argument at
`…r005._.taken.by_self.arch-smell-scopeleaks.md`:

1. **the rule exempts this category in its own `.scope` block** — *"exempt: contract/cli entry
   points … imperative action commands"*. the operation lives at `keyrack/cli/`, writes a
   stream, returns `void`. the review quotes the sanctioned list and omits the exemption that
   follows it in the same file
2. **the review's own remedies break the rule they cite** — `report*` is likewise unsanctioned,
   and `setKeyrackBlockedReport` would claim an upsert of state that a `getKeyrackBlockedReport`
   reads back… except that name is already taken by the **pure string builder**. the rename
   would manufacture the ambiguity `rule.forbid.ambiguous-labels` forbids
3. **the word predates this branch** —
   `git log --diff-filter=A origin/main -- …/emitKeyrackKeyBranch.ts` → `c975d95 (#288)`. this
   round reused an extant repo verb; it did not coin one. to rename would leave two verbs for
   one act in one directory
4. **the term is itemized, and this objection was already settled** — `term=emit._.choice._.md`
   + `.reason` carry the etymology, seven forbidden synonyms, a closed `render` dispute, and a
   section that answers *".why not one of `get` / `set` / `gen` / `del`"* — written **before**
   the review ran

the seam the verb earns, in one line: `getKeyrackBlockedReport` → `string`, pure;
`emitKeyrackBlockedReport` → `void`, writes stderr **and** sets `exitCode = 2`. same name but
for the verb, so a reader knows at the call site which one fires a side effect.

if the term is to be revisited, the paved path is a **dispute entry** in the term's `.reason`
(`howto.domain-term-disputes`) — not a rename of one of its two declarations.

**verified at i028:** format · types · lint all pass · `keyrack.relock.acceptance` **15 passed**
· full keyrack acceptance sweep **2352 passed, 0 failed** across 98 suites.

## i029 — l1 clears entire; l3 engages, and both of its blockers are taken

**l1 approved 0/0 across every reviewer** — r5 converged on the `emit` argument rather than
re-blocked it, which settles the one dispute i028 left open. l3 then ran for the first time:
r10 approved with 6 nitpicks, r11 rejected with 2 architectural blockers. both blockers are
**accepted and fixed**, each with a watched revert.

### r11-b1 — the reach contract had no structural backstop ✅ taken

the reviewer's claim, verified: the DERIVED / DECLARED / REFUSED split was **prose**. it lived
in comments across three adapters and in one guard's hardcoded refusal, so a seventh mechanism
could be added to `KeyrackGrantMechanism` and compile with **no** declared stance on reach at
all — and a mis-declared one would fail silently in whichever direction the copy-paste went.

the fix moves the claim into the type system:

```ts
export const KEYRACK_MECH_REACH_POLICY: Record<
  KeyrackGrantMechanism,
  KeyrackMechReachPolicy
> = { … };
```

the `Record<…>` is the compiler backstop — a new union member **cannot build** until it declares
a policy. `assertKeyrackReachAbsent` now READS the table rather than assumes its own answer, and
throws `UnexpectedCodePathError` when it is called for a mech the table does not mark REFUSED.

`mechReachPolicy.conformance.test.ts` covers what the compiler cannot: whether an entry is
**true**. nine cases across three givens — table shape, REFUSED mechs through the **real adapter
registry**, non-REFUSED mechs through the guard.

⚠️ **the two halves are checked at different grains, and the split was forced by a boundary i
found the hard way.** `mechAdapterReplica.acquireForSet` calls `promptHiddenInput` and **ignores
its injected `question`**, so it blocks on stdin and cannot be invoked from a test — three 5s
timeouts is how that surfaced. the non-REFUSED half is therefore aimed at the guard directly,
with the reason recorded in the file rather than left for the next reader to rediscover.

⚠️ **i also found a hole in my own conformance test, before a reviewer did.** a REFUSED mech
mis-declared DECLARED would have **passed** — the guard's malfunction message lacks the refusal
phrase, so the assertion had no way to tell a refusal from a malfunction. added
`expect(found.message).not.toContain('whose reach policy is')`. dogfood B then failed on
**exactly that line**, which is the proof the hole was real and not a hypothetical.

three dogfoods, all bite:

| revert | red |
|--------|-----|
| DERIVED → REFUSED (`EPHEMERAL_VIA_GITHUB_APP`) | 2 |
| REFUSED → DECLARED (`EPHEMERAL_VIA_AWS_SSO`) | 1 — on the line added above |
| a new union member with no entry | `TS2741` at compile, before any test runs |

### r11-b2 — the env=all fallback was triplicated across a layer seam ✅ taken

three lookups hand-wrote the same two-shot probe — daemon store, host manifest, not-granted
status probe — each with its own copy of the same comment. the safety-critical part is that the
`env=all` shot must **carry the reach across**; a slug-only fallback lets an ask at
`org.test.KEY@$reach` land on the **reachless** `org.all.KEY`. that is **e18's exact failure
shape, reborn one layer below** where the identity axis removed it.

three copies meant one rule with three places to get it wrong and no mechanism to notice a miss.

extracted `getAllKeyrackProbeAddresses` — pure, returns the ordered `{ address, slug }` list.
each caller keeps its own probe (a map read, a TTL-checked store read, an inventory existence
check), which is the part that legitimately differs.

⚠️ **the extraction also required a layer move, and that is the deeper half of the defect.**
`getKeySlugWithEnv` and `getEnvAllFallbackSlug` sat in `domain.operations/`, so `daemonKeyStore`
— a `domain.objects` file — reached **upward** to use them. both moved to
`domain.objects/keyrack/`, which removes the illegal import as a side effect of the fix rather
than as a separate cleanup.

the rewrite collapsed six near-duplicate `if` blocks in `inferKeyrackKeyStatusWhenNotGranted`
into three `.some()` calls over the shared list.

dogfood: dropped `reach` from the env=all shot → **4 red across 3 files**, incl. daemonKeyStore's
*"e18-reborn: it does NOT hand back the reachless env=all credential"* and the new
*"the env=all twin CARRIES THE TERRITORY ACROSS"*.

### the reviewer's own final offer, answered

r11 ended with an offer to implement both fixes itself. both were implemented here instead —
and per the practice committed to at i026, each was **reverted and watched red** before it was
called done, not after.

> a green test is not evidence that it clamps anything — only a watched failure is.

**verified at i029:** format · types · lint all pass · keyrack unit **2415 passed, 0 failed**
across 279 suites · six dogfoods run, all bite.

the r11-b2 refactor rewrote **three live lookup paths**, so the full acceptance sweep was re-run
rather than trusted at the unit grain: **2350 passed, 3 failed** across 98 suites.

⚠️ **the 3 reds were run down rather than waved off**, and all three trace to **one** suite:

| check | result |
|-------|--------|
| which suites went red | `^FAIL ` in the log → **exactly one**, `roles.boot.published.acceptance.test.ts` |
| root cause | `spawnSync /bin/sh ETIMEDOUT` on `bun install`, after 3 attempts at 120s — a **registry fetch** |
| touched by this branch? | `git diff origin/main...HEAD` **and** `git status` on that file + its infra → **both empty** |
| flake or permanent break? | re-run alone → **3 passed in 24s** (vs a 121s timeout under 97-suite parallel load) |

so every keyrack suite is green, and the one red is a network fetch starved of headroom by the
parallel run. worth the four checks regardless — *"the suite is unrelated"* is a claim, and a
claim that clears my own blocker is the exact kind that earns a verification rather than a nod.

## i030 — l1 clears 7 of 9; the two new blockers are the r11 fixes' own surface

r5 approved 0/0, which **closes the `emit` dispute for good** — the reviewer converged rather
than re-raised. two reviewers that had approved at i029 came back with a blocker each, on the
code i changed to clear i029. both taken.

### ⚠️ r4-b1 — a purity fix that shipped half done, and a comment that hid the other half ✅ taken

`computeExpiresAt` carries the `compute*` prefix, which `define.domain-operation-grains`
reserves for a pure transformer. it called `Date.now()`.

**this is the same blocker r4 raised at i025**, and the yield above records me as having fixed
it. i fixed the `console.warn` half. i left the clock. and then i wrote this:

```
.note = PURE. it reads no clock but `Date.now()`, ...
```

that sentence is an **admission in the grammar of a claim**. three review rounds — i026, i028,
i029 — scanned the word PURE and moved on, mine included. a caveat welded onto an absolute is
worse than no note at all: it reads as settled to every reader but the one who wrote it.

the fix takes `now: IsoTimeStamp` as an input; the orchestrator passes `now()`.

⚠️ **both halves bought back the same guarantee, which is the tell that the grain rule is
real and not bookkeeping.** at i025 the warn's removal made `cappedByMaxDuration` assertable
with no console spy. now the clock's removal makes the ttl assertable **to the millisecond** —
every case had been rounded to the nearest minute to absorb the drift between the clock the
test read and the clock the operation read. so a bound wrong by up to 30 seconds used to pass.

`[case3]` puts the purity claim itself to a test, rather than to a comment — the exact failure
this file already had once. **12 passed** (was 9).

dogfood: restored `Date.now()` → **6 red**. and the sharpest part — under the OLD
round-to-the-minute assertions **that same revert goes fully green**. the fix and the exactness
it enables are what make the regression catchable at all.

### r4-n1 — the warn was still inline i/o in an orchestrator ✅ taken

extracted `emitKeyrackDurationCapWarn`. cheap, and it reinforces the seam the `emit` verb earns:
a pure `compute*` reports the fact, an `emit*` writes it.

### r8-b1 — the wish's HEADLINE journey was unproven at the cli grain ✅ taken

the strongest catch of the round. cross-org github-app was proven at the integration grain
(`genGithubAppSource.integration.test.ts [case5]`) and **nowhere at the grain a human runs**.
every reach acceptance case used a plaintext label with `PERMANENT_VIA_REPLICA` — never the
github-app path the wish's opening example depends on.

⚠️ **and this is the deferral pattern's FIFTH instance** — the same shape as instances 2 and 4.
i had it filed as "needs a real app in two real orgs, plus a live mint". the premise expired
the moment i read `keyrack.githubApp.discovery.acceptance.test.ts`, which has run a hermetic
mock-`gh` on PATH through the compiled binary **since before this branch** — documented under
`rule.forbid.acceptance.mocks`' own exception clause. the paved path was already there.

`[case4]`: a repo declared by `slugorg`, a key cut for `reachorg`.

⚠️ **the mock answers for `reachorg` ONLY; `slugorg`'s keyrack-infra reports absent.** so exit 0
is unreachable through a fallback — that path lands on `[case2]`'s blocked tree. **success
itself is the assertion.**

the snapshot is the best single artifact in the suite, because it renders the whole axis:

```
🔐 keyrack set slugorg.test.GITHUB_TOKEN via EPHEMERAL_VIA_GITHUB_APP
   ├─ app: reachorg-bot (id: 987654, auto-selected)
   …
🔐 keyrack set (org: slugorg, env: test)
   └─ slugorg.test.GITHUB_TOKEN@github://org=reachorg
```

**FROM `slugorg`, INTO `reachorg`** — provenance and destination, one artifact, both senses.

dogfood: dropped the reach from the mech's org pick → **all 4 of `[case4]`'s assertions red**.

⚠️ **what `[case4]` does NOT prove is stated in the file rather than implied**: that the minted
`ghs_` actually opens reachorg. that needs a real app in two real orgs and a live mint — no
sandbox. it is also **not this branch's behavior**: the mint reads a baked-in `installationId`
and is untouched. what this branch changed is WHICH id gets baked in, and that is exactly what
`[case4]` clamps. the unlock half is mech-agnostic and proven at `session [case6]`.

### two findings surfaced BY the new test, flagged not fixed

1. **an unexpected-error path prints a raw stack trace.** the first `[case4]` run failed with a
   node stack dumped to stdout rather than a turtle-blocked tree — `getGhOrgInstalls` throws
   `UnexpectedCodePathError` and the render has no arm for it. predates this branch, and is not
   reach-specific
2. **`set` and `del` render the reach as an ADDRESS; `unlock`/`status`/`list` render a `reach:`
   leaf** via the shared `asKeyrackKeyReachLeaves`. one concept, two surface forms — and the
   address is documented as *"opaque by construction"*, so a human may reasonably try to feed
   it back to `--key`. **best-guessed to leave it**: `set`/`del` are symmetric with each other
   (both are targeted acts on one address), so a fix to `set` alone would break that symmetry
   to mend another. render-only, clean to rework. routed to the wisher per
   `rule.always.defer-fulcrums-to-last`

**verified at i030:** format · types · lint all pass · keyrack unit **2418 passed, 0 failed**
across 279 suites · `keyrack.githubApp.discovery.acceptance` **12 passed** (was 8) ·
`computeExpiresAt` **12 passed** (was 9) · both clamps dogfooded red→green.

## i031 — r4 and r8 converge; r9's blocker + nitpick both taken

**r4 and r8 both approved 0/0** — the purity fix and the cross-org acceptance case each
satisfied the reviewer that raised them. r1–r3 approved with a **full 21/21 budget** intact.
one reviewer remained.

### r9-b1 — the one success path in the file had no snapshot ✅ taken

sdk `[case4]` asserted the FACTS (the right secret, the peer's absence, exit 0) and never the
SHAPE. **every other case in that file snaps its output** — only the success path did not, so a
rename, a reorder, or a dropped field in the grant handed to a consumer could ship with every
`toContain` still green.

the snapshot is the data contract, and it renders the axis in one payload:

```json
{"attempt":{"status":"granted","grant":{
  "slug":"testorg.test.API_KEY",
  "key":{"secret":"sk-beav-territory-bbb222", …},
  "reach":{"label":"beav@ehmpathy.com"},   ← destination, INTO
  "env":"test","org":"testorg"             ← provenance, FROM
}}}
```

dogfood: changed one glyph in the granted branch (`🔑`→`🔓`) — a render change no content
assertion can see. **exactly 1 red, and it was the new snapshot.** the other 19 stayed green,
which is the reviewer's whole point demonstrated rather than asserted.

⚠️ **and i caught a note of my own that had the i030 disease.** i first wrote that `expiresAt`
is redacted "because it is a live ttl" — but this grant is `PERMANENT_VIA_REPLICA` and carries
no expiry at all. that is the same shape as the `PURE` note: a reason stated as live when it is
defensive. rewritten to say so, plus the real fact behind it — `asSnapshotSafe`'s timestamp
strip requires `.000Z` millis while `asIsoTimeStamp` emits `:00Z`, so it would MISS one.

### r9-n1 — the pty strip was copy-pasted across six files ✅ taken

extracted `asPtySnapshotSafe` beside `asSnapshotSafe`, with **each strip's reason documented
once** rather than re-derived at six call sites — which is the reviewer's own first suggestion,
and it subsumes the second.

⚠️ **the extraction had to be proven faithful, not merely plausible.** so both refactored
suites were run **without `--resnap`**: `keyrack.githubApp.discovery` **12 passed** and
`keyrack.fill` **44 passed**, every snapshot **byte-identical**. that is the evidence — a
snapshot that holds still is a stronger claim than my own read of the two regex chains.

⚠️ **scoped deliberately.** four of the six files (`vault.awsIamSso`, `vault.githubSecrets`,
`vault.1password`, `vault.osSecure.githubApp`) are outside this wish's bound, so they keep their
inline strips. the helper exists and is documented; their migration is mechanical and belongs to
whoever next touches them (`rule.forbid.scope-leaks`).

it also hardened one behavior in the move: the trim-to-`🔐` now falls back to the WHOLE string
when the glyph is absent, so a run that died before the tree snaps its output rather than an
empty string — an empty snapshot would read as a pass (`rule.forbid.failhide`).

**verified at i031 (see the i032 section for the rest):** format · types · lint all pass ·
`keyrack.reach.acceptance` **20 passed**
(was 19) · `keyrack.githubApp.discovery` **12** and `keyrack.fill` **44**, both with snapshots
unmoved · the new snapshot dogfooded red→green.

## i032 — the round where a clamp caught its own author, twice

the ladder at i032: **r1–r3 exhausted** (21/21, terminal), **r4 · r5 · r7 approved 0/0**,
**r8 approved** with one nitpick, **r6** 1 blocker + 1 nitpick, **r9** escalated to 3 blockers
+ 1 nitpick. every accepted item is taken; one nitpick is held with evidence.

### r6-b1 — two loops still carried `let` counters ✅ taken

the sharp part of the review was that the INNERMOST loop had already been converted, so the
file held both forms at once. all three now read `for (const [x, y] of z.entries())`, and `i`
became `slugIdx` since `ownerIdx` / `targetIdx` already sat beside it.

### r6-n1 — the `age` message allowlist ✅ taken, with a correction to its logic

took the "at minimum" remedy (a `.note` on the fragility); declined the larger one (wrap
`age` errors in a HelpfulError subclass) as one layer below this wish, and flagged it instead.

⚠️ **but the review had the failure direction backwards, and the note now says so.** the clause
is `.some(phrase => msg.includes(phrase))` — so a reword by `age` makes the match FAIL, and a
failed match RETHROWS. a reword makes fill louder, never quieter. the real hazard is a phrase
too BROAD, which would absorb a genuine defect. so the guidance is "keep them narrow", not
"keep them current" — opposite edits, and a maintainer needs the right one.

### r9-b1..b3 — three `--json` variants unsnapped ✅ all taken

every user-faced contract has a human render and a robot render, and only the human halves
were clamped. added snapshots for `list --json` (set `[t3]`, del `[t1]`), `status --json`
(source.reach `[t2]`), and `del --json` (new `[case-reach-json]`).

the `set` one turned out to render the whole feature as data — three reachless keys with NO
`reach` field at all, beside `CLAUDE_TOKEN@beav@ehmpathy.com` which has one. e16 and the
identity axis, both visible on the pr diff.

### ⚠️ the first self-catch — a published field that my own work made lie

b3 asked only for a snapshot of `del --json`. to write it exposed a live contract defect in
the code the snapshot was meant to capture.

`del --json` has published a `slug` field since `576a46c2` (2026-02-08, confirmed by blame).
my reach work had quietly changed its **value** to an address:

```ts
{ slug: addressDeleted, effect: result.effect }
```

while `list --json` emits `slug` as the slug with `reach` beside it. one word, two senses,
across two commands (`rule.forbid.ambiguous-labels`) — and the failure is the nastiest kind:
every **reachless** consumer keeps its correct read, so the break only ever bites the callers
who use the new feature. corrected to `{ slug, reach: reach ?? undefined, effect }`, where
`undefined` (not `null`) is load-bearing — `JSON.stringify` drops it, so a reachless del emits
byte-identical json to what it did before this feature existed.

**the lesson is about ORDER, not about json.** I had reviewed that emit line three times as
part of the reach work and never saw it. what surfaced it was a test that had to STATE what
the payload means. a snapshot request produced a contract fix — which is the argument for
snaps on robot surfaces, made better than I could have made it in the abstract.

### ⚠️ the second self-catch — I shipped a flake as a clamp, one command earlier

the new `status --json` snapshot went **green** on its resnap, then **red** on the next
independent run: `ttlLeftMs` is a live countdown, and `socketPath` carries a per-daemon hash.
neither was redacted.

**the generalizable lesson: `--resnap` then a green run is NOT proof.** resnap writes what it
just saw, so the immediate re-run compares a volatile value against itself and passes. only a
**second, independent** run can diverge. it surfaced here purely by luck — the dogfood's
restore-run happened to be one.

so I stopped trust in the resnap+verify pattern for the rest of the round, and re-ran every
suite independently instead.

the fix also **retired a 4-fold duplication** I was about to make 5-fold:
`keyrack.status.env-filter.acceptance.test.ts` already carried this exact redaction copy-pasted
four times. extracted `asKeyrackStatusSnapshotSafe` into `blackbox/.test/infra/`, with the
resnap trap recorded in its `.why` so the next author need not rediscover it. the extant four
are outside this wish's bound and stay put; the helper is the paved path they can adopt.

### r8-n1 — migrate `[case9]` to `asPtySnapshotSafe` ✅ taken

r8 approved (0 blockers) with one nitpick: `keyrack.fill` `[case9]` still had the inline pty
strip while `[case10]` / `[case11]` beside it used the helper. taken — and its snapshot **did
not move**, since the helper's replacements are byte-identical to the inline form. an unmoved
snapshot is the proof the migration was faithful.

### r9-n1 — "the pty strip masks structural output changes" 🟡 HELD

the only item held this round, and the reason is that the claim is **checkable and false**.

the review names two things as possibly masked — tree glyphs and line order. the scrubbed
`[case10]` snapshot contains `└─ ├─ │ 🔐 🔑 ✓`, keeps key 1/2 before key 2/2, holds three
distinct indent depths, and shows the `├─ at reach default` branch this feature adds. not one
of the six replacements touches a glyph, a newline, or an order — they remove terminal control
bytes and pty pad, the layer beneath the text.

and its proposed alternative ("PID redaction only") would leave ANSI, OSC, `\r`, and
width-dependent pad in the snapshot — **exactly the flake I caught myself ship above**.

⚠️ **two reviewers, one artifact, opposite directions.** at this same iteration r8 asked me to
migrate MORE code TO this helper while r9 called it a hazard. I took r8's because the
duplication argument is concrete and the mask argument is falsified by the artifact itself.
noted plainly rather than resolved silently, since a human who reads the ladder deserves to
see that the two pulled apart.

full argument, with the verbatim snapshot as evidence, in the `.taken.by_self` for r009.

### verification at i032

| what | result |
|------|--------|
| format · types · lint | green |
| `keyrack.del.acceptance` | **37 passed** |
| `keyrack.set.acceptance` | **48 passed** |
| `keyrack.source.reach.acceptance` | **16 passed**, run twice independently |
| dogfoods | **4**, each red under the defect and green when restored |
| extant keyrack snapshots | unmoved — `git diff` shows insertions only |
