## i033 — the l3 blocker closed, and two regressions the sweep found

this round took r11's single blocker and r10/r11's shared nitpick list, then ran the whole
keyrack suite **at every grain** rather than only the files I had touched. that last choice is
what turned the round: **two real regressions surfaced that no scoped run could have seen**,
and both were mine.

### r11-b1 — `env=all` + reach has zero acceptance coverage ✅ CLOSED

both l3 reviewers ranked this first; r11 blocked on it. the invariant is the one
`getAllKeyrackProbeAddresses` calls "the single most safety-critical of the reach axis": the
`env=all` fallback must CARRY THE REACH ACROSS, or an ask at `org.test.KEY@$reach` lands on the
REACHLESS `org.all.KEY` — e18's exact failure shape, one layer below where the identity axis
removed it.

it was proven only at `daemonKeyStore.test.ts [case10]`, **in memory**, against a store built
by direct `.set()` calls. that unit cannot see a reach dropped on the way IN (cli parse, slug
construction, socket serialization) or OUT — every one of which leaves it green while the wire
hands back the wrong secret.

new: `blackbox/cli/keyrack.reach.env-all.acceptance.test.ts` (**9 passed**) +
`with-keyrack-reach-env-all` fixture. it mirrors `[case10]`'s three claims one for one and
drives each through the real cli → socket → daemon wire with `get --value`, so every assertion
is about the **secret a caller receives** rather than a stored object.

⚠️ **the dogfood is the strongest evidence in this whole build.** I reverted shot 2 of
`getAllKeyrackProbeAddresses` to a slug-only fallback:

```
[t1] reach ask for beav@ehmpathy.com  → received sk-all-env-reachless-ccc333  ← WRONG TERRITORY
[t2] reach ask for an UNCUT territory → exit 0, handed the reachless secret   ← e18, alive
```

a wrong-territory credential, handed back silently, exit 0. that is verbatim the failure the
wish says would "defeat the whole feature" — and until this file existed, no acceptance test in
the repo could see it.

### r10/r11-n3 — `keyrack list` reach coverage ✅ taken

`list` is the POSSESSION audit — "which territories does this machine hold keys for?" — and it
answers from the HOST MANIFEST, so it is the only command that can answer for a machine whose
keys are all locked (the ordinary case). the render was proven in isolation; the manifest READ
was not.

new `[case-reach]` in `keyrack.list.acceptance.test.ts` (**14 passed**), no unlock, plus the
robot half: two distinct address-keyed entries, each entry reports its SLUG not its address,
and e16 clamped with `'reach' in entry` rather than `toBeUndefined()` — the latter passes on the
one shape e16 forbids. the snapshot renders the vision's rack aha exactly: two branches, one
name, one `reach:` leaf. `[case9]` beside it did not move.

### r10/r11-n5 — e15 ordering ✅ taken, and the vision's wording corrected

new `[case6]` in `genGithubAppSource.integration.test.ts` (**15 passed**), hermetic.

⚠️ **the vision's e15 says the pem error "fires BEFORE any network call". that is stale.** it
was written when the vision still assumed a per-unlock installation lookup; q7 moved the lookup
to set time, where it now runs FIRST (`genGithubAppSource.ts:54`). so I clamped what is actually
true and actually matters: a reach introduces no failure of its own ahead of the pem, and leaves
no residue behind it.

the residue half is the sharper claim, and it is reach-specific: **a reach chooses WHICH org's
`keyrack-infra` gets written.** a registry write before the pem read would leave an authorization
trail in a THIRD PARTY's repo for a credential that was never stored. dogfooded by a move of
`genKeyrackInfraRegistryGithubApp` above the pem read → an orphan `ehm-a-seaturtle` entry
appeared in ehmpathy's registry, exactly as predicted.

### ⚠️ REGRESSION 1 — I widened a probe allowlist and it swallowed the error that named the fix

`fillKeyrackKeys [case6]` went red in the full integration sweep:

```
expected: message contains 'nonexistent'
received: UnexpectedCodePathError: hostManifest required for set { slug: testorg.test.API_KEY }
```

the original fill probe absorbed four **message phrases**. I replaced it with
`isKeyrackFillProbeMiss`, which is class-based — and had to be, because e6's reach miss is a
`ConstraintError` and a `prefer` reach could never be provisioned otherwise. but "any
caller-fixable error" is strictly wider than those four phrases, and it swallowed **"I cannot
read this owner's vault at all"** — which is not an answer to "is this key vaulted yet?".

result: a caller who typos `--owner` used to get an error naming the owner; they now got an
`UnexpectedCodePathError` (exit **1**, a defect code) thrown from deep inside `setKeyrackKeyHost`
for what is plainly a typo (`rule.require.exit-code-semantics`).

**this is exactly the hazard I articulated one iteration earlier**, when I corrected r6's
reasoning about the `age` allowlist: *"a reword makes the match FAIL → RETHROW, louder not
quieter. the real hazard is a phrase too BROAD."* I wrote that down, then widened an allowlist
in the same file family and did not re-derive the consequence.

**fixed at cause, not at the allowlist** (`rule.require.solve-at-cause`). the root is that
`daoKeyrackHostManifest.get` returns **null** for an absent manifest rather than throws, so
fill's extant "verify all owners BEFORE user input" fail-fast never fired. added the missing
guard there, deliberately OUTSIDE the try so the catch cannot rewrap it into `no available
prikey` and bury the `rhx keyrack init --owner …` hint. the probe's class-based allowlist is
untouched, so e6 still works.

`[case6]` gained two clamps beside its extant one: the error is a `BadRequestError` (exit 2,
not a defect), and its metadata names the fix. dogfooded — all three go red with the guard
disabled.

### ⚠️ REGRESSION 2 — a required-field contract change left two assertions on the old shape

`vaultAdapterGithubSecrets.integration.test.ts` ×2:

```
  Object { "keySlug": "ehmpathy.test.API_KEY",
+          "mech": "PERMANENT_VIA_REPLICA" }
```

I made `mech` **required** on `acquireForSet` (deliberately — an adapter is not 1:1 with a mech,
so an adapter that guessed its own identity would name the wrong one in every refusal a human
reads). the vault passes it correctly. but the two assertions still asserted `{ keySlug }` alone,
and **the compiler cannot see that**: the mocks are `jest.fn()`, so `toHaveBeenCalledWith` takes
`any`.

fixed by assertion of the full shape, with a note on each stating why `mech` must be there.

### ⚠️ the meta-lesson, and it is the same one twice

both regressions were invisible to every scoped run I did, and both surfaced the moment I swept
the whole suite. neither file was one I had edited this round.

that is `feedback_scope_matched_evidence` from the other side: I had been checked at the scope
of my **edits**, when the claim I owed was about the **feature**. a contract change (`mech`
required) and a helper substitution (`isKeyrackFillProbeMiss`) both radiate past the files they
live in, and the only run that can see that radius is the full one.

### one failure that predates the branch, flagged not fixed

`invokeRun.stdin.integration.test.ts` ×2 — `no skill "read-stdin" found in any linked role`,
while the error lists THIS repo's skills. a role-discovery/cwd issue in the test infra; the file
and every prod file on that path are untouched by this branch. out of the wish's bound.

## i034 — the r10 items, all three closed

r11 approved at i033 (0 blockers). r10 rejected with 2 blockers + 1 nitpick. all three landed,
each dogfooded.

### b1 — `fill --repair` / `--allow-dangerous` × multi-reach was untested

the blocked-key branch and the reach axis were each proven alone, never together — and they
intersect where it counts: the branch decides on `checkGrant`, which is now asked **per target**.
a reach-blind probe there would let one territory decide every territory's fate.

landed: `[case10]` (`--allow-dangerous` → `summary.skipped === 3`) and `[case11]` (`--repair` →
`summary.set === 3`) in `fillKeyrackKeys.integration.test.ts`, both on a shared
`genDangerScene({ caseName })` that seeds three addresses with distinct `ghp_dangerous_*` values
straight into the host manifest + `os.direct` vault. seeded by hand because a fill **cannot**
produce this state — its roundtrip `get` returns `blocked` and `assertKeyrackFillRoundtrip`
throws.

the dogfood matrix is the durable part:

| what I broke | `[case9]` | `[case10]` | `[case11]` |
|--------------|-----------|------------|------------|
| drop the reach from the per-target `getKeyrackKeyGrant` | 🔴 | 🟢 | 🔴 |
| drop declared reaches from `getAllKeyrackFillTargets` | 🔴 | 🔴 | 🔴 |

`[case10]` cannot see the first break — with a reach-blind `checkGrant` every target reads the
same blocked verdict and `--allow-dangerous` skips all three either way. so **`[case10]` clamps
the ENUMERATION and `[case11]` clamps the ADDRESSING**. as a pair they cover the branch; alone,
neither does. that is now in the file, so a future reader who deletes one does not assume the
other still guards the same ground.

⚠️ one shape note earned in the process: a mixed `string | null` array sorts by `String(null)`,
which lands `'null'` between `'beav'` and `'vlad'`. the per-territory attribution maps the
reachless entry to the word `'reachless'` for that reason, with a note on why.

### b2 — the discovery mock had no `.real` counterpart

`rule.forbid.acceptance.mocks` demands a documented `.real` beside an unavoidable mock, and the
header carried a `.why-mock` sentence in its place — a justification for the mock, not a statement
of where the truth is checked.

replaced with a `.real` that names **two proofs at two grains**:

1. **the `gh` boundary** — `runGh.integration.test.ts`, in CI
2. **the cross-org mint** (does a `ghs_` minted at reach X really open org X?) — ⚠️ proven by no
   automated test, and it cannot be, since it needs a second org with the app installed. so the
   `.real` carries the exact three-command manual proof, and calls out that a `404` (not a `403`)
   is the tell: github hides an unreachable repo rather than forbids it

the second entry is the one that matters. an honest `.real` that says "this is unverified, and
here is how a human verifies it" beats a tidy one that implies coverage nobody has.

### n1 — `github.secrets` had no reach clamp of its own

`assertKeyrackReachAddressable` is called by both `os.envvar` (read) and `github.secrets` (write).
with a test at only one call site, a `direction` typo — or the guard deleted from one — leaves the
other vault's suite green. a shared guard needs a clamp **per call site**.

landed: `[case4]` in `vaultAdapterGithubSecrets.integration.test.ts`, two claims:

- it refuses **by class** (`ConstraintError`), message names both vault and territory — by class,
  so a reword of the copy cannot move the clamp
- **the mech is never asked to acquire.** this is the claim with teeth: a guard placed after
  `acquireForSet` would walk a human through a whole guided setup and *then* refuse, and for the
  github-app mech that setup writes to a real registry

dogfooded by a move of the assert below `acquireForSet`: the first claim stayed green, the second
went red. exactly the split I wanted.

## i035 — `del`'s raw refusal, the ttl clamp's absent wire test, and a type that lied

r10 **approved** (0 blockers, 6 nitpicks). r11 **rejected** with 1 blocker. this round closed the
blocker and r10's two highest-ranked nitpicks, and found two defects on the way in.

### the r11 blocker — accepted, but its evidence was wrong on three counts

the conclusion held: `del` was the one command whose refusal rendered as a **raw class dump**,
where `set` / `source` / `unlock` each render the turtle tree
(`emitKeyrackBlockedReport` at `invokeKeyrack.ts:797`, `:1020`, `:1363` — and nowhere in `del`).

but every path the review cited to prove it "live" was wrong, and I verified each:

| the review claimed | the code says |
|---|---|
| `github.secrets.del` throws a reachable `ConstraintError` | **unreachable.** `delKeyrackKeyHost:63-64` returns `not_found` unless a host entry exists at the composite address — and `setKeyrackKeyHost` calls `adapter.set` (`:100`) **before** it persists (`:202`), so a refused reach-set writes no entry to find |
| `os.envvar.del` reaches the same guard | it throws `UnexpectedCodePathError` as its **first statement** (`:143-147`) — a defect class, correctly rethrown |
| the proposed `catch (e instanceof ConstraintError)` fixes it | it would have caught **no reachable error at all** |

that last row is the one that mattered. the two refusals a human actually hits are in the handler
itself (`:1203`, `:1210`) — a mistyped slug org, an `--env` that disagrees with its slug — and
both threw `BadRequestError`. since `ConstraintError extends BadRequestError`,
`instanceof ConstraintError` is **false** for them. a verbatim apply would have read as done while
the defect stayed put.

**what landed instead:** both guards promoted to `ConstraintError` (which is what earns exit **2**
and what `emitKeyrackBlockedReport` accepts by type), each given a `hint`, and the guards placed
**inside** the try alongside the domain call.

⚠️ and a mislabel caught mid-write: `getKeyrackBlockedReport:40-41` renders `metadata.slug` under
the label **`repo:`** — a key written for the infra errors, where `slug` means a github repo. my
first draft passed `slug: opts.key`, which would have printed a keyrack slug as a repo. named
`key` instead.

**the clamp:** `[case4]`/`[case5]` already covered both paths — and passed for the whole life of
the defect, because `not.toEqual(0)` plus a loose phrase regex is satisfied by a stack trace that
happens to contain the phrase. strengthened to exit **exactly 2**, the tree present, no class name
leaked, the hint present. dogfood: one guard reverted to `BadRequestError` → **3 red**.

### r10 nitpick 2 — the e17 ttl clamp had zero coverage above the pure function

the reviewer ranked this its own highest risk, and it is mine by my own rule. verified:
`grantExpiresAt` appears in exactly three places — the pure function, its unit test, and the call
site. **no test at the `unlockKeyrackKeys` grain, none in blackbox.** so a regression that dropped
`grantExpiresAt: grant.expiresAt` would leave every test green while `status` again advertised
`540m` for a token github kills at 60 — the hazard the vision calls an acceptance-breaker.

landed: `[case-ttl-clamp]` with three claims —

| t | claim |
|---|---|
| `[t0]` | a 9h ask against a mech that reports 55m yields ~55m, not 540m |
| `[t1]` | the **daemon** holds the clamped value too — `status` reads the store, so a clamp on the reply alone would still lie later |
| `[t2]` | **e1**: a mech with no self-life leaves the requested 9h untouched — a clamp that read an absent `expiresAt` as "expires now" would break every permanent key, and `[t0]` alone would not notice |

`genMockVaultAdapter` gained an optional `grantExpiresAt`, absent by default, so every extant
caller behaves byte-identically.

**dogfood:** dropped `grantExpiresAt` from the call site → **exactly `[t0]` and `[t1]` red,
`[t2]` green**. the clamp bites *and* discriminates.

### ⚠️ a type that lied, found because it bit me

`DaemonKeyRow.expiresAt` was declared `number`. the daemon stores and compares it as an **iso
string** — deliberately, since iso stamps sort lexicographically
(`daemonKeyStore.ts:65-67, 102`).

no caller was broken, because each passes the value to `new Date(...)`, which takes either. but
`number` invites `expiresAt - Date.now()` — which compiles, yields `NaN`, and is exactly what my
first draft of `[t1]` wrote. the test failed against **correct code**.

the declaration came from `origin/main`; I inherited it when I extracted the inline type into
`DaemonKeyRow` — and then wrote a `.why` comment that **asserted the lie as fact**. that is my
`trust-but-verify` failure: I read the type and repeated it rather than checked the runtime. the
comment is now the correction, per `rule.require.timeless-comments`, and the field is
`IsoTimeStamp` (`rule.require.shapefit`: a type that does not fit is a defect whether or not it
has bitten).

### a second proxy trap, same family as i033's

`useBeforeAll` hands back a **proxy**, and a proxy passed to `new Date(...)` yields `NaN` rather
than a timestamp — so my first `genSelfLife` made the clamp read as absent. replaced with a plain
function, called per test, which also pins the 55m window to the moment of the unlock. noted in
the file so the next author does not re-derive it.

| `unlockKeyrackKeys.integration` | **11 passed** (was 8) |
| keyrack **unit** (279 suites) | **2418 passed, 0 failed** |
| keyrack **integration** (95 suites) | **869 passed, 2 failed** — the same `invokeRun.stdin` pair, confirmed by name in the log |
| dogfoods this round | **2**, each red under the defect and green when restored |

### still open, flagged for the wisher

r10's residual nitpicks are coverage-depth asks, each proven at a lower grain today: **e10**
peer-isolation at the cli grain (proven in `daemonKeyStore.test.ts`), **q1** relock dedupe at the
cli grain (proven at the store), **e20** the ci `os.envvar` throw (proven at the guard), and
**e23** — whether the export-collision guard is reachable through any current caller, or exists
only for a caller yet to be built. that last one is a design question, not a test gap, and belongs
with the wisher.

## i036 — r10's four residual nitpicks, triaged against source. two were wrong.

rather than take the four on the reviewer's word, each was checked against the code. the result
is the useful part: **two of the four claims are false**, and the two that hold are now closed.

| r10 item | verdict | evidence |
|---|---|---|
| **#3** e10 re-unlock idempotency at cli | ⚠️ **valid** | `keyrack.session [case6]` proved isolation on a FIRST unlock only; no case re-unlocked |
| **#4** q1 relock dedupe "no acceptance test found" | ❌ **FALSE** | `keyrack.relock [case3]` exists, relocks by **`--env`** (the sweep, not `--key`), and clamps both the `pruned` count and totality. its own `.note` records that I first wrote `--key`, found it clamped less than it read like, and moved it — for the reviewer's own reason |
| **#5** e20 `os.envvar` reach-ask at cli | ❌ **FALSE** | `blackbox/sdk/keyrack.reach [case3]` drives a keyed reach-ask down to `os.envvar` on the built contract, and asserts **absent** + exit 0 + no peer value |
| **#6** e23 guard may be dead code | ✅ **TRUE** | independently confirmed — see below |

### #6 — the e23 reach branch IS unreachable today, and the comment said otherwise

verified through both callers:

- `source --key` builds a **single-element** array (`invokeKeyrack.ts:717-729`) — one attempt
  cannot collide with itself
- the sweep goes `getAllKeyrackGrantsByRepo` → `getAllKeyrackSlugsForEnv`, which returns
  `string[]` of **bare slugs**, and asks `getKeyrackKeyGrant({ slugs })` with **no reach** — so
  every attempt it yields is reachless, and `labelOf(a) === labelOf(b) === null`

so the guard fires today only on the **env** axis (the `--env all` case, which predates reach).
the comment above the call site claimed the reach case was live — a
`rule.require.timeless-comments` defect, since a future reader would believe it reachable.
corrected to name which axis is live, plus why the reach branch stays: it costs one label
comparison, and the repo manifest **already** declares `reaches: require|prefer` (q8) that a
reach-aware sweep will enumerate.

### #3 — closed, and the dogfood corrected my own note

`keyrack.session [case6] [t2]` now re-unlocks one territory with the **reachless peer unlocked in
between**, so the re-unlock cannot pass merely because the store sat untouched.

⚠️ I labelled the peer assertion "THE clamp" and the dogfood disagreed. reverted
`daemonKeyStore.set` to `store.set(grant.slug, …)`:

| assertion | under slug-only storage |
|---|---|
| `the territory still answers a get` | 🔴 red — the peer unlock overwrote the one row, so the **reach** key is what was lost |
| `its REACHLESS peer is untouched` | 🟢 green — the peer is exactly what survived |

so the two halves catch **opposite** regressions and neither implies the other. the note now says
which is which, with the dogfood recorded.

### ⚠️ the same lesson, a third time

my previous pass called #5 "valid" after a grep of **`blackbox/cli/`** — while the claim was about
the whole blackbox surface, and the case that refutes it lives in `blackbox/sdk/`. that is
`feedback_scope_matched_evidence` again: **a check narrower than the claim it supports**. i033 had
it on edits-vs-feature, i034 on the glossary headline, and here on a directory. the shape is
always the same — the fix is to derive the search scope from the CLAIM, never from wherever I
happen to have my eyes.

### one stale line in the vision, flagged not fixed

the vision's **e20** says a reach-ask on `os.envvar` "throws". the shipped design is narrower and
better: the vault throws for a **direct** ask (unit-clamped), while a multi-source **probe** treats
it as an absent source — because a throw there killed the whole `fill` run for a `require` reach,
the very scenario the feature exists for. absence is an answer, not a fault. the vision's phrase
predates that correction.

## i037 — `get`'s raw refusal, and the relock sweep path `[case3]` did not cover

r11 **approved** (0 blockers). r10 rejected with 1 blocker. its prose says *"no new blockers
found"*, so the tally landed on its one `[UNADDRESSED]` item — which was real, and is fixed.

### the blocker — `get` rendered TWO error shapes, inches apart

`invokeKeyrack.ts` line **473** renders the turtle tree for a `--reach` misuse. lines **493-503**,
immediately below it, raw-threw `BadRequestError` for the three plainest usage faults there are:
`--value` without `--key`, neither `--for` nor `--key`, and a bad `--for` value.

so the same command answered two ways, and the shape a human is **most** likely to meet — the one
they hit the first time they type the command from memory — was the raw one. this is the identical
defect I fixed for `del` at i035, one command over.

all three now render the blocked tree with exit 2 and a named fix. the snapshot moved, and the
diff is the proof:

```
- BadRequestError: must specify --for repo or --key <slug>
- [args] keyrack,get
+ 🐢 bummer dude...
+ 🐚 keyrack get
+    └─ ✋ blocked: must specify --for repo or --key <slug>
+       └─ hint:
+          ├─ one key — rhx keyrack get --key $KEY
+          └─ every key in this repo — rhx keyrack get --for repo
```

resnapped **+7/-3**, then verified on an independent run (162 passed) per
`rule.require.snapshot-verified-on-independent-run`.

**the clamp:** `keyrack.get.output [case4]`'s two whens had the same weak shape `del`'s did —
`not.toEqual(0)` plus a loose regex, both satisfied by a stack trace. strengthened to exit
**exactly 2**, tree present, no class name leaked, hint present. dogfood: one guard reverted to the
raw throw → **5 red**.

### the relock gap was real, and narrower than I had checked

r10 caught that `handleRelockCommand` has **two** sweep paths, not one: the `--env` filter
(`:35-42`) and the bare no-args clear (`:45-51`). they are separate code that share a shape, and
`[case3]` drove only the first.

⚠️ this is the i036 lesson turned back on me. I had recorded #4 as **FALSE** on the evidence that
`[case3]` exists and uses `--env` — which is true, and answers a *narrower* claim than the one that
mattered. the reviewer's claim was about the sweep **paths**; my check was about the sweep **flag**.
same failure mode as the `blackbox/cli`-vs-`blackbox/sdk` grep, one round later.

`keyrack.relock [case4]` now drives the bare sweep against one slug held at two territories, and
clamps both the dedupe count and the totality of the clear.

### a refinement to i036's #5, which was too flat

I recorded e20 as **FALSE** because `blackbox/sdk/keyrack.reach [case3]` covers a reach-ask that
reaches `os.envvar`. that case proves the **probe** path — absence is reported, no peer value is
handed back. the reviewer's claim is about the **direct-ask throw**, which is genuinely unit-only.
two different behaviors on one vault; my "false" answered one of them. the residue is real and
stays open.

## i038 — e20 bound at the contract grain, and a reviewer malfunction diagnosed

r10 **approved** at 0 blockers / 6 nitpicks. r11 **malfunctioned** — no verdict to answer.

### the last of the three "closed" hazards finally has an acceptance test

r10's ranked #1 was the one residual I had already conceded as real at i037: e20 / q9 — the
`os.envvar` direct-ask refusal — was proven **only** at unit grain. two independent reviewers
named it in a row, which is the signal that a conceded gap should stop to be conceded.

**the fixture:** `with-keyrack-reach-envvar` declares `vault: os.envvar` for BOTH the reachless
key and its territory, so the unlock reaches that vault **by declaration**, not by fallback. that
is what makes it a *direct* ask rather than the probe. fully hermetic — an env var is the whole
vault, so no daemon, no disk store, no credential, no network.

**`blackbox/cli/keyrack.reach.envvar.acceptance.test.ts`**, 11 assertions across three whens:

| when | what it clamps |
|------|----------------|
| `[t0]` unlock names a territory | exit **exactly 2**, the turtle tree, no class name, `os.envvar` named, snapped |
| `[t1]` a keyed reach-ask reads the value | the ambient value is **never** handed back; the territory reads `absent`, never `granted` |
| `[t2]` the same unlock, reachless | e1 — exit 0, key unlocked, tree byte-identical |

### two snapshot/label defects I caught in my own test, before the reviewer could

**⚠️ the first snapshot captured a blank string.** I snapped `result.stdout` for `[t0]` — but a
refusal is not a result, so the tree renders on **stderr** and this key's stdout is empty. the
snapshot was `"\n"`. it would have held green through *any* change to the refusal at all. moved to
`result.stderr`; the snapshot now carries the full blocked tree, its `why`, and its `hint`.

**⚠️ the second was the [case6] mislabel, repeated verbatim.** I wrote `// THE clamp` over
*"the ambient reachless value is never handed back"* — and the dogfood proved it stays **green**
with the guard torn out, because `unlock` renders a status tree and never prints a secret. the
assertion could not make the claim its comment made.

the fix was not to relabel but to **make the claim true**: `[t1]` now issues a second invocation
with `--value`, which is a genuine raw-secret read. the third dogfood confirms it — with the guard
removed, that assertion goes **red**.

> this is the third time a comment of mine has named a clamp that could not bite, and every one
> was caught by the dogfood rather than by a re-read. the dogfood is not a formality; it is the
> only instrument that can tell an assertion from a decoration.

### the dogfood, three passes

| pass | guard | red |
|------|-------|-----|
| 1 | torn out | 3 — exit code, tree, snapshot |
| 2 | torn out, after `[t1]` added as a status read | 4 — `absent 🫧` flipped to **`granted 🔑`** for a territory no key was cut for |
| 3 | torn out, after `[t1]` gained `--value` | **6** — the value-grain clamp among them |

pass 2 is the one worth a second look: without the guard, a territory that was **never cut**
reports `granted`. that is e18 rendered on screen, and it is precisely the wish's headline hazard.

### r11's malfunction — diagnosed, cause found, driver-fixable

r11 produced a full prose review and then **ended with a question to me** rather than a verdict, so
it emitted no numeric tally. the guard reached for the prose-tally sub-brain, which
*"could not be reached"*.

one bounded diagnosis pass, per `rule.always.diagnose-reviewer-malfunctions`:

```
$ rhx keyrack status --owner ehmpath        # 4 keys live, 539m — creds are NOT expired
$ rhx keyrack get --owner ehmpath --key FIREWORKS_API_KEY --env prep
   └─ ehmpathy.prep.FIREWORKS_API_KEY
      └─ status: locked 🔒
```

the tally sub-brain runs on fireworks (`define.brain-creds-codepath`), and its key was **locked**.
that is the whole cause, and it is mine to fix rather than the human's — `rhx keyrack unlock
--owner ehmpath --env prep --key FIREWORKS_API_KEY`, now held for 540m.

⚠️ worth a note for the next traveler: the malfunction message names the *symptom* ("sub-brain
could not be reached") and not the *cause* (a locked credential, with a `keyrack unlock` that would
fix it). a reader who took the message at face value would suspect the network or the reviewer.
that is `rule.require.errors-name-the-fix` unmet in the guard's own output — flagged, out of scope
here.

### r11's substantive question, answered

> *"want me to fix the shared mech-adapter registry now, or leave it as a flagged follow-up?"*

**leave it.** r11 verified the point itself: 2 of the 6 duplicated files have **zero diff** against
`main`, so the duplication predates this branch entirely. to fix it would mean edits to files this
pr does not otherwise touch — scope creep past the wish's `.scope: ehmpathy/rhachet only` and past
one-pr-per-worktree. it stays on the wisher's flagged list, where it has been since i031.

### one r10 nitpick is stale, and this round is why

r10 wrote that e20's cli coverage was *"deliberately skipped with an inline note … a documented,
reasonable tradeoff"*. that note is now **gone**, and the coverage is real. the same is true of its
bare-`relock` item, which `[case4]` closed at i037 — r10 read a tree one iteration behind on both.

## i039 — the host manifest gets its round-trips, and a blocker answered on the evidence

r10 approved again. r11 **tallied cleanly** — the fireworks unlock at i038 fixed the malfunction,
and r11's own successful tally is the proof. it rejected: 1 blocker, 3 nitpicks.

### q12/a4 is closed — two cases, opposite directions

the residual I named at i038 as *"the single largest untested claim on the branch"* is now bound,
and r11 independently named its twin in the same round. both landed:

**`[case14] a legacy pre-reach manifest on disk`** — the bytes an OLDER rhachet wrote, as a
hand-authored JSON literal, encrypted and read back through the real decrypt → parse → hydrate path.

⚠️ the literal is the whole point. every other case in that file builds its fixture through
`dao.set`, which serializes a **current**-shape dobj — so it can only ever round-trip today's bytes.
a legacy file is one this codebase can no longer produce, so it must be authored by hand. the
coverage that already existed (`schema.test.ts`) was zod alone on a plain object, which cannot see
the hydrate loop, `asKeyrackKeyReachField`, or `assertKeyrackHostAddressed` — each of which runs
only on this path and each of which could reject a legacy entry while the schema test stayed green.

two entries, on purpose: `LEGACY_FULL` is a manifest written just before reach landed;
`LEGACY_MINIMAL` also omits `env`, `org`, `meta`, `maxDuration` — the OLDEST shape — so the defaults
path is exercised alongside the absent-reach path.

**`[case15] a reach-bearing entry round-tripped through the dao`** — the POSITIVE twin, which r11
caught was absent.

⚠️ the blind spot it closes is genuinely subtle, and worth the record: the two reach cases that
already existed are **both negative**. `[case13]` proves a misaddressed entry HALTS; `[case14]`
proves an absent reach STAYS ABSENT. neither can catch a dao that simply **drops** the territory on
the way out — `[case13]` goes green (no address left to conflict with) and `[case14]` goes green
(it asserts absence, which a drop produces). two tests that each look like they cover it, and a hole
between them.

`[case15]` writes a reach-bearing entry AND its reachless peer of the same slug. the peer is
load-bearing, not decoration: absent it, "the reach survived" would also hold for a dao that stored
one entry and lost the other.

### the dogfoods, both with teeth

| clamp | defect introduced | red |
|-------|-------------------|-----|
| `[case14]` — an absent reach stays absent | absent reach defaulted to `null` on hydrate | **1**, exactly the assertion that names the claim |
| `[case15]` — the reach-bearing entry keeps its label | territory dropped on hydrate | **5**, incl. the label claim and `[case13]`'s guard |

pass 2 is instructive: to drop the reach also breaks `[case13]`, because the misaddress guard has no
reach left to conflict against. the two cases catch genuinely different things, which is the proof
they are not redundant.

### r11's blocker — answered on evidence, and my prior answer sharpened

r11 blocked on the mech-adapter registry duplicated 6× with no compiler link. its diagnosis of the
**hazard** is correct and I did not dispute it. what I disputed is that it belongs in this pr — and
this time with a check at the right grain:

```
$ git diff origin/main -G 'mechAdapters' --stat -- src/domain.operations/keyrack/adapters/vaults/
(no output)
```

**zero files.** across all six vault adapters this branch modifies **not one line** that matches
`mechAdapters`. every duplicated registry is byte-identical to `origin/main`.

⚠️ r11's own scope check was **file**-level (*"2 of the 6 have zero diff"*) and had gone stale —
the branch has since touched five of the six, to add reach guards. a file-level check now reads as
"the branch is all over these files", which is true and answers the wrong question. the line-level
filter answers the right one. this is the scope-matched-evidence lesson again, and this time I
applied it before I made the claim rather than after.

**r11's strongest argument is the one that draws the line most clearly.** it says the repo already
solved the identical shape correctly in `KeyrackMechReachPolicy` — a complete `Record` plus a
conformance test. that registry **is this branch's work**. the branch applied the pattern to the
registry it *introduced*; it did not retro-apply it to one that predates it and that it never
touches. "fix the pattern where you introduce it" is the discipline; "fix every prior instance you
happen to stand near" is how a bounded pr becomes an unbounded one.

and the hazard cannot regress here: the failure mode needs a **7th mech**, and this wish adds none —
`KeyrackGrantMechanism` has the same six members it had on `main`.

## i041 — the `prefer` reversal: option (a), by the wishers direction

**the wisher cut the strength tier and kept the declaration.** verbatim:

> "i want to have the reach in the repo manifest guide the fill / just like we have a
> is-optional-if-has: xyz parser / this option; (a) drop the words, keep the declaration —
> `reaches:` stays in the repo manifest, every entry becomes unconditional. removes
> `KeyrackKeyReachDirective` + `KeyrackFillTarget`s directive field."

reached after four rounds of my own questions on the pair — *"explain when it would ever be
`prefer`. why wouldnt it always be require?"* → the `fill` answer → *"that makes sense"* →
*"yeah drop it though"* · *"yagni"* · *"keep it simple"*. so the pair was understood before it was
cut, which is why the reversal is recorded rather than merely applied.

### what the surgery removed

| deleted | why it existed |
|---------|----------------|
| `KeyrackKeyReachDirective.ts` | the dobj — `{ directive, reach }` + the closed word set |
| `isKeyrackFillSkippable.ts` (+ test) | the soft-arms error allowlist, to keep its catch from a failhide |
| `asKeyrackFillSkipReason.ts` (+ test) | the `🟡 skipped preferred reach` render |
| `fill`s outer try/catch | the soft arm itself — every target now propagates |
| `FillKeyResult.status: skipped-preferred` | the third outcome |
| `summary.skippedPreferred` | the fourth bucket |

**six parts for one word no manifest had said yet.** that ratio is the whole argument for the
cut, and it is why I take the wishers call as correct rather than merely obeyed.

### the one judgment I made inside the instruction

`isKeyrackFillSkippable` had **two** consumers, not one — the soft-arm catch *and*
`isKeyrackFillProbeMiss`. so a naive delete would have broken the probe. I inlined its single
line (`error instanceof BadRequestError`) into the probe, with its full reasoning comment moved
intact. the probe is now the only place that names "caller-fixable", which is the honest shape:
one consumer, one definition, no seam to drift.

### the clamp that carries the most weight

⚠️ **an old-shape manifest must HALT, never be read leniently.** the map form sat in real repos,
and two lenient readings were both plausible and both wrong: flatten the values (right
territories, silently stale file) or read the keys (territories literally named `require` and
`prefer`). `[case11][t2]` asserts the refusal.

**dogfooded, and it bit:** I wrote the lenient `Object.values(...).flat()` a permissive author
would write. exactly one test went red — that one. restored → green.

### the acceptance A/B, repurposed rather than deleted

`[case10]` used to prove the soft arm skipped; it now proves the LAST declared territory halts.
`[case11]` proves the FIRST one does. together they claim what neither claims alone: **position
does not grade a territory.**

**dogfooded:** I restored a strength tier in `fill` and rebuilt. both arms went red, on exactly
the assertions that carry the change — exit code and the absence of the skip render. restored →
43/43 green.

the strongest single line is `not.toContain(is skipped preferred reach)`: it clamps the **absence
of a feature**, which an exit-code assertion alone cannot express.

### verification at i041

| what | result |
|------|--------|
| format · types · lint | green |
| keyrack **unit** (277 suites) | **2407 passed, 0 failed** |
| keyrack **integration** (95 suites) | **878 passed, 2 failed** — both `invokeRun.stdin`, pre-branch |
| keyrack **acceptance** (50 files) | **1516 passed, 0 failed** |
| dogfoods this round | **2**, each red under the defect and green when restored |
| snapshots moved | 6 lines in 1 integration snap · 2 acceptance snaps rewritten · 1 unit snap · all exactly the suffix removal or the halt |

⚠️ **4 daemon-timeout failures appeared in the first 95-suite integration run and are contention,
not regression** — re-run narrowly, all green. named rather than waved past: `[case3]`,

### glossary

`prefer` and `directive` are **RETIRED, not deleted** — both rewritten to lead with a status
block that names what the slot was, why it was declined, the deleted paths, and the condition
under which the verdict flips back. `directive` additionally KEEPS the declared-vs-derived line
it draws against `grade`, which was never about reach.

### flagged for the wisher — new this round

`fill`s halt renders as a bare `BadRequestError: ... [args]` dump rather than the turtle-blocked
tree that `get` / `set` / `unlock` use. **pre-existing**, and it was already visible in
`[case11]`s prior snapshot — but it is now visible in TWO snapshots rather than one, because the
soft arm used to swallow the last one. out of this wishs bound; named so it is not mistaken for
a regression.

## i042 — `fill/` gets its own subdomain dir

**the wisher asked why the fill operations sat at the broad `keyrack/` root. they should not
have, and the placement was my defect** — `rule.prefer.most-common-denominator` says create an
operation at the most specific place it serves, and lift only on proven reuse. I created four
branch-new operations at the root by default rather than at their home.

verified before the move, not assumed:

- **`keyrack/` already holds subdomain dirs on main** — `adapters`, `cli`, `daemon`,
  `getKeyrackKeyGrants`, `getKeyrackKeySecrets`, `grades`, `infra`, `recipient`, `session`. so
  `session/unlockKeyrackKeys.ts` is a direct precedent: an orchestrator that lives inside its
  own subdomain dir beside the leaf operations it owns
- **the four are fill-only in production** — the sole non-fill reference is a test
  (`assertKeyrackReachAddressable.test.ts`, which clamps the probe pair)
- **`git ls-tree origin/main` shows `fillKeyrackKeys.ts` + its integration test exist on main;
  all four leaf operations are branch-new** — so the root placement is mine to correct, and to
  correct it now is the cheap moment

**moved (11 files, via `rhx mvsafe`):**

```
fill/fillKeyrackKeys.ts + .integration.test.ts
fill/assertKeyrackFillRoundtrip.ts + .test.ts
fill/getAllKeyrackFillTargets.ts + .test.ts
fill/getOneKeyrackFillTargetCount.ts + .test.ts
fill/isKeyrackFillProbeMiss.ts + .test.ts
fill/__snapshots__/fillKeyrackKeys.integration.test.ts.snap
```

**deliberately NOT moved: `cli/asKeyrackFillTargetBranch.ts`.** it is fill-only too, so
most-common-denominator reads toward `fill/` — but it is a **renderer**, and every keyrack
renderer lives in `cli/`. a reader who wants "how does keyrack draw a tree?" looks in one
place; to scatter renderers by feature would cost that more than the locality gains. the
render subdomain wins over the feature subdomain for a render.

**the move is inert, and that is the claim to prove:**

| check | result |
|-------|--------|
| types · lint | green |
| keyrack **unit** (277 suites) | **2407 passed, 0 failed** — identical total to pre-move |
| `fill` **integration** | **13 passed, 0 failed** — snapshot resolved at its new path |
| `npm run build:compile` | tsc + tsc-alias + both bun binaries |
| keyrack fill **acceptance** | **43 passed, 0 failed** |
| snapshots moved | **zero** — jest keys snapshots by test NAME, and the snap file travelled with its test |

zero snapshot movement is the strongest available evidence the move changed no behavior: a
rename that altered any rendered byte would have shown up here.

**imports repaired:** 11 relatives inside `fillKeyrackKeys.ts` and 1 in its integration test
went `./` → `../` (the four true siblings stayed `./`); 4 outside consumers re-pointed —
`invokeKeyrack.ts`, `assertKeyrackReachAddressable.test.ts`, and
`cli/asKeyrackFillTargetBranch.ts` + `.test.ts`.

⚠️ **the index does not yet reflect the move** — `git.stage.add` refuses without stage
permission, so `git status` shows the old paths as staged-then-deleted and `fill/` as
untracked. the **worktree** is correct and every gate above ran against it; to stage is the
human's call at commit time.

## i043 — r10's two items, worked

r10 (`enroll-impl-behavior-intent`) flipped **approved → rejected** against the i041 tree, with
1 blocker + 1 nitpick. I verified both against live state rather than take the transcript. the
full articulation is at
[`…r010._.taken.by_self.enroll-impl-behavior-intent.md`](./.reviews/peer/5.1.execution.from_vision._.review.i041.e427c8b69c381e992c.r010._.taken.by_self.enroll-impl-behavior-intent.md).

### 🔴 the blocker is a constraint the human fixed, not a gate I can work

verified: `origin/main` is **7 commits** ahead (the reviewer's count and its named commits are
exact). the rebase this demands needs a commit floor, and the wisher ruled **"no commits.
finish your drive."** — so per `rule.always.converge-to-terminal` this is a `constraint` the
human accepted knowingly, terminal rather than convergeable. recorded in the withdrawn blocker.

### 🟡 the nitpick — bulk `source` is reach-blind, silently

**the item is right and sharper than "friction":** a bare `keyrack source` emits the reachless
credential, exits 0, and says no word about a territory held beside it. the vision's own spine
is that no keyrack path drops a territory silently.

**the obvious fix is a trap, and I caught it before I shipped it.** my first move was a
manifest-derived warn — `context.repoManifest` is already in hand, so it costs no I/O. I read
the fixture first:

```yaml
# blackbox/.test/assets/with-keyrack-reach-source/.agent/keyrack.yml
env.test:
  - API_KEY          # ← declares NO reaches
```

the flagged scenario's reach key was cut **ad-hoc** via `unlock --reach` — q8 pass 1's whole
point, that an ad-hoc reach is host-side and appears in no manifest. so a manifest-derived warn
would be **silent in the exact case reported** while it reads as a guard: a check that cannot
bite, which is the defect `rule.require.clamp-edge-cases` forbids in a test.

**what shipped** — the reviewer's own offered close, on the command description:

```diff
- .description('output export statements for shell eval')
+ .description('output export statements for shell eval (a bare sweep sources reachless keys only)')
```

### ⚠️ and the first attempt at it was WRONG — the tests caught a real regression

I first put the hint on the `--reach` flag description and dropped its examples. that broke
**two explicit assertions**, not merely a snapshot:

```
✕ help says what a reach names, in plaintext        → lost 'beav@ehmpathy.com'
✕ help also demonstrates the cross-org github form  → lost 'github://org=ehmpathy'
```

`keyrack.reach.help` clamps a **uniformity invariant**: all five reach-flagged commands carry
the SAME `--reach` description, so a human learns the label shape once. its own `.note` records
that two commands once shipped without the github form and it was deliberately made consistent.
my edit would have re-broken exactly that.

**so the hint moved to the command description**, which is where it belonged anyway: the
asymmetry is a fact about *this command's sweep*, not about what the flag means. flag
uniformity preserved, hint delivered at the top of `--help`. the argument is a `.note` at the
declaration so the next author does not re-litigate it.

I also left a durable `.note` at the emit loop that names the omission, why the runtime warn is
deferred (it needs the daemon's full held list — a socket round-trip on a path that is eval'd
into a shell), why the manifest cannot stand in, and the condition under which it should land:
**with a reach-aware sweep, where the held list is already in hand.**

⚠️ **the gap is not closed and I do not claim it is** — the runtime silence remains. what
changed is that it is announced where a human looks and recorded where the next author works.

### ⚠️ an unrelated red found by the full sweep, and repaired

the 100-file keyrack acceptance sweep returned **2422 passed / 2 failed**, and neither failure
was mine:

```
✕ init --roles incremental [case20] multi-add summary tree
✕ init --roles incremental [case22] comma-form add summary tree
      ├─ 13 brief(s)  →  16 brief(s)
      └─  2 skill(s)  →   3 skill(s)
```

**diagnosed rather than resnapped blindly.** `git diff origin/main -- package.json` shows this
branch bumps **`rhachet-roles-bhrain` 0.31.3 → 0.32.1**, and the reviewer role gained 3 briefs
+ 1 skill in that release. the snapshot file is **byte-identical to `origin/main`** —
untouched by this branch — so the bump landed with no resnap beside it, and these two have been
**red in CI on this branch ever since**, independent of all reach work.

resnapped; the diff is exactly 4 lines, the two count pairs and no more. **flagged for the
wisher:** the bhrain bump itself is unexplained by any reach work and may be incidental —
worth a look at merge time.

## i045 — the invariant hand-duplicated 4×, closed structurally

**r11 flipped APPROVED → rejected again**, with a genuinely new structural defect that 42 prior
iterations had missed. it is a good catch and i took both halves of it.

### 🔴 the "`--reach` requires `--key`" rule, hand-authored 4×, each worded its own way

four independent copies of one invariant:

| site | its own text |
|------|--------------|
| `unlockKeyrackKeys.ts` | *"requires --key … in an env"* |
| `getKeyrackKeyGrants.ts` | *"requires a key selector … in a repo"* |
| `invokeKeyrack.ts` (`get`) | *"requires --key … in a repo"* |
| `invokeKeyrack.ts` (`source`) | *"requires --key. a reach names one territory of one key."* |

the drift is the proof of the defect — four copies, four texts, already diverged. and this repo
already structurally prevents exactly this pattern elsewhere (`assertKeyrackReachAbsent`,
`assertKeyrackReachAddressable`, `assertKeyrackHostAddressed`).

**fixed:** `assertKeyrackReachRequiresKey` holds the rule once; all four sites call it.

the split is the part worth a record. the **message** is shared — that is the drift the reviewer
found. the **hint** stays per-caller, because a hint's job is to be copy-pasteable and the fix is
command-shaped (`rhx keyrack get --key …` vs `rhx keyrack unlock --env $env --key …`). to share
the hint would trade a drift hazard for a useless hint (`rule.require.errors-name-the-fix`).
`keyed` is a plain boolean because the four callers hold their selector in four different shapes.

### 🔴 the 5th site — and my own prior claim was wrong

⚠️ **i told r10 at i044 that "`relock` has no `reach` axis at all." that was false.** true of
`relockKeyrack.ts` and the cli — which is where i looked — and **false of the daemon layer**,
where `daemonAccessRelock.ts:37` and `handleRelockCommand.ts:37` both carried an unanchored
`reach?`. a `{ env, reach }` or bare `{ reach }` was legal TypeScript that **silently dropped
the filter**, in the one operation whose job is to revoke.

**the lesson, and it generalizes:** i derived my search scope from **the surface i had touched**
rather than from **the claim i was making**. the claim was about `relock` as a whole. this is
the same failure mode the command-surface table (q13) was authored to prevent — an instrument
that samples reads exactly like one that sweeps.

**fixed at rung 1, not rung 3.** the reviewer offered a runtime guard or a union and named the
union as better; i took the union:

```ts
| { slugs: string[]; reach?: KeyrackKeyReach; env?: undefined }
| { slugs?: undefined; env?: string; reach?: undefined }
```

`{ env, reach }` and bare `{ reach }` are now **unrepresentable**
(`rule.prefer.prevent-over-correct`).

**and the union immediately earned itself** — it broke `relockKeyrack.ts:42`, which forwarded
`slugs` and `env` as a flat pair for the callee to unpick. the documented `slugs > env > all`
priority is now spelled at the call site, one branch per shape.

**a runtime guard was kept at `handleRelockCommand` too, and that is not belt-and-braces:** that
operation is fed parsed JSON off a **socket**, which no compile-time union constrains. it throws
`UnexpectedCodePathError` rather than an error aimed at a human, because no cli path can produce
the shape — so it can only arrive from a client defect, and a defect surfaces loudly rather than
dressed as a constraint (`rule.require.exit-code-semantics`).

### 🟢 three dead barrel lines, deleted

`domain.operations/keyrack/index.ts` re-exported three casts from `domain.objects` with **zero**
consumers — verified independently before deletion (`invokeKeyrack.ts` and `sdk.keyrack.ts` both
import directly; the published `./keyrack` subpath goes through `sdk.keyrack.ts`).
`rule.forbid.barrel-exports`.

### 🟢 r10's #3 — `e19/e21/e22 × --reach`, closed

two integration cases added to `genGithubAppSource.integration.test.ts`:

- **`[case7]`** (e21) — no org has a reachable `keyrack-infra`, so a lookup at *either* org
  throws. what is under test is **which org the refusal names**, because the error names the org
  in both its message and its hint (`rhx keyrack infra init --org $org`)
- **`[case8]`** (e19/e22) — the reached org's registry is empty; **the slug's own org is fully
  seeded**. so a fallback would **succeed** and hand back a blob. the mere presence of an error
  is itself evidence the reach drove the lookup — this case cannot go green by accident

### a parse-order fix, surfaced indirectly

`keyrack source` parsed its reach *after* the key check while `keyrack get` parsed *before*, so
a malformed label reported as an absent `--key` — the less specific of the two fixes. reordered.

### dogfoods this round — 3

| clamp | under the defect | restored |
|-------|------------------|----------|
| `relockKeyrack [case4][t1]` — env absent, not merely `undefined` | **1 red**, 6 green | 7 green |
| `genGithubAppSource [case7]`/`[case8]` — lookup follows the reach | **8 red**, 4 reachless green | 20 green |

the first is the one worth a read: jest's `toHaveBeenCalledWith` treats an absent key and an
`undefined` one alike, so `[t0]` alone **cannot** tell "env dropped" from "env: undefined". the
dogfood shows exactly that — `[t0]` stayed green while `[t1]` went red.

the second showed the same lesson from the other side: `[case7]`'s *"it fails loud"* assertion
stayed **green** under the defect (both orgs lack infra), which is why the org-name assertion
sits beside it. a lone "it throws" clamp there would have had no teeth.

### four assertions moved with the unified message

2 cli acceptance, 1 sdk acceptance, 1 unit, plus one renderer fixture. each **updated** to the
new text rather than loosened — the sdk one especially, since
`not.toContain('--reach requires a key selector')` would have stayed green against a message
that no longer says "selector", which is a clamp that quietly loses its teeth.

| `build:compile` (tsc + 2 bun binaries) | green |
| keyrack **acceptance** (100 suites) | **2427 passed, 0 failed**, 24 skipped |

### the acceptance verdict, taken from its terminator

⚠️ the human corrected a real hazard mid-round: i had read a **polled partial buffer**
(`💤 inflight (1200s)`) and was one step from a claim it could not support. a tree with no
terminator is **incomplete, never empty**. the run's own final line, verbatim:

```
   │  └─ 🎉 passed (1328s)
   ├─ stats
   │  ├─ suites: 100 files
   │  ├─ tests: 2427 passed, 0 failed, 24 skipped
   │  └─ time: 1328s
   └─ log
      ├─ omitted on success by default
      └─ hint: use `--log always` to persist when desired
```

**and it cross-checks.** the pre-resnap run of the identical scope reported
`2424 passed, 3 failed`. `2424 + 3 = 2427`, so the three snapshot fixes landed **and** no test
was lost or pruned — the half a bare "0 failed" would have hidden, given that `--resnap` can
silently delete a snapshot whose test threw before it was reached.

captured as `rule.require.skill-terminator-for-verdicts`. ⚠️ **a peer authored the same lesson
concurrently in another worktree**; the two briefs must be reconciled to one home at merge.

### two reds diagnosed rather than resnapped

|-----|---------|
| `invokeRun.stdin.integration` × 2 | **not mine.** the test, `@src/.test/infra`, and the skill-discovery path are **untouched** by this branch (`git status` lists only keyrack files); it fails identically in isolation; and `main`'s CI is green. a local-environment/hermeticity red, flagged rather than fixed on an unrebaseable branch |
| the 150-line `fillKeyrackKeys` snapshot "deletion" | **a file MOVE, not a prune.** verified: the new copy under `keyrack/fill/__snapshots__/` is 150 lines, the exact count |

## i046 — r11 APPROVED · r10's last round, and an error i found in my own note

**r11 (`enroll-impl-arch-defects`) flipped rejected → APPROVED**, 0 blockers, 18/21. the shared
assertion + the union converged it. **r10 rejected at 20/21 — one round of budget left.**

### 🔴 the blocker is the wisher's, and the reviewer says so itself

r10's one blocker is the bulk-`source` reach-blindness, and its own recommendation routes it:

> *"This is the one item worth a decision from you before merge"* … *"surface … to the wisher for
> an explicit ratify/reject"*

that is the escalation `rule.always.converge-with-reviewers` names as legitimate — a scope
question above the driver's authority. i did not re-decide it. but i spent my own levers first,
and one of them caught a real defect **in my own prior argument**.

### ⚠️ the note said the right thing for an incomplete reason

the code note justified "no runtime warn" with: *"the repo manifest cannot stand in — an ad-hoc
reach is host-side and appears in no manifest (q8)."*

re-read, that looks like a hole, because **the HOST manifest is precisely where ad-hoc reaches
live**. so a host-manifest warn would be zero-I/O and would close the blocker outright. i went to
implement it.

**it does not work** — `ContextKeyrackGrantGet` carries **no host manifest by design** (*"avoids
host manifest decryption and passphrase prompts for get operations"*), so to consult it here
would force a **decryption and a passphrase prompt onto a path whose stdout is eval'd into a
shell**. strictly worse than the round-trip it was meant to dodge.

so the conclusion held and the **stated reason did not** — incomplete in exactly the way that
invites the next reader down the path i just walked. the note now rules out **both** sources
explicitly. this is trust-but-verify applied to my own prior work, and it is why the check earned
its hour even though it changed no behavior.

### 🟢 the `status` reach leaf — misfiled coverage, closed

the only acceptance proof of a `status` reach render lived inside the **relock** suite. that is
fragile in a specific way: a future edit that narrowed the relock suite would take the `status`
proof with it, **and the deletion would look unrelated in the diff**.

**`keyrack.status.acceptance.test.ts [case7]`** — key cut for one territory, unlocked, read back
through `status`; asserts the key is on the rack **and** that the territory is named.

**dogfooded:** reach leaf removed from `asKeyrackStatusKeyBranch`, rebuilt → **exactly 1 red**
(`the territory it opens is named`), 57 green. restored → **58 green**.

### 🟡 the one nit i cannot close

r9 `ergo-friction-hazards` is `exhausted 🌙` at 21/21, so its lens cannot re-run on this stone.
its rebuttal stands unre-adjudicated, and i said so plainly rather than manufacture a substitute.
flagged for the wisher.

## i047 — the ladder reaches terminal; r11 converged to `approved`

### what moved

| reviewer | i046 | i047 |
|----------|------|------|
| r11 `enroll-impl-arch-defects` | rejected — 0 blockers, 2 nitpicks | **approved** ✓ (575.7s, 20/21) |
| r10 `enroll-impl-behavior-intent` | 1 blocker, 1 nitpick | `exhausted 🌙` at 21/21 — terminal |

**every one of the 11 reviewers is now terminal: 8 approved, 3 exhausted (r6, r9, r10).** the
route's own gate now asks for a human — `--as approved` or a budget extension. that is the
route's checkpoint, not one i invented (`rule.always.drive-autonomously`).

### the work that converged r11

**B2 — the export-collision guard, wired at all 3 flatten sites.** the census found worse than
the review reported: the three sites did not merely lack a guard, they held **three different
collision semantics** — cli **threw**, `sourceAllKeysIntoEnv` kept the **FIRST**, and
`getKeyrackKeySecrets` kept the **LAST**. one collision, three answers, two of them silent.

i declined the prescribed *"shared attempts → collision-checked map primitive"*, on evidence:
only **one of the three** sites returns a map (the cli emits shell lines, the sdk mutates
`process.env` in place). what they share is the **invariant**, not the output — so the invariant
is what got extracted, with a shared **message** (cannot drift) and a per-caller **hint** (a
`--reach` hint on an sdk path names a flag that cannot be typed).

**dogfood #5** — removed the `input.hints?.` fallbacks: `12 passed, 1 failed`, red at exactly
`[case5][t0]` and nowhere else; `[case5][t1]` stayed green, which proves the clamp is specific
rather than a blanket break. restored: `13 passed, 0 failed`.

### ⚠️ earned exhaustion — the cited record owed by `rule.always.converge-to-terminal`

three reviewers went terminal by exhaustion, not approval. each is accounted for:

| reviewer | what was attempted | what would fix the review |
|----------|--------------------|---------------------------|
| **r10** `enroll-impl-behavior-intent` (1 blocker) | the blocker is the bulk-`source` reach-blindness, which **the review itself routes to the wisher** ("above the driver's authority", i033→i046). i spent my levers on the nitpick instead, and repaired a real defect in my own prior justification — the `.why not a warn` note had ruled out the daemon round-trip but left the **host manifest** unaddressed, which is the more seductive wrong road | the reviewer keeps a blocker open across 14 iterations that it has itself declared out of driver scope. a review lens that can mark a finding **"escalated, not actionable"** would let the level converge instead of burn budget to re-report a product decision |
| **r9** `ergo-friction-hazards` (3 blockers) | rebutted with evidence; the rebuttal was never re-adjudicated because the budget ran out first | budget exhausted mid-conversation. the level needed one more round to read the rebuttal, not more work from me |
| **r6** `arch-hazards-maintenance` (1 blocker) | addressed in its `.taken` at i032; no re-run available | same shape as r9 |

**the honest read:** not one of the three exhausted on a defect i declined to fix. two exhausted
because the budget ended mid-conversation, and one because it re-reports a decision it has itself
routed elsewhere. that is the accountable tail, not a coast.

### ⚠️ three instrument defects found this round — all one failure class

each returns an honest-looking result over the wrong set, which is `rule.forbid.failhide` in tool
form:

1. **`grepsafe --glob 'src/**/*.ts'` → `matches: 0`** — a glob that names a directory silently
   matches no files. `rule.forbid.grepsafe-path-globs` already documents this; **i violated it
   three times** and concluded "zero skips repo-wide", which was false. re-run with
   `--glob '*.ts' --path <dir>`
2. ~~**`--scope path://` is only-first-wins** — a second path scope is silently dropped while the
   header echoes both. a stacked run would have reported a clean `0 failed` for a sweep that
   never touched a reach suite~~

   ⛔ **RETRACTED 2026-08-06 — the claim was false, and its refutation is the better lesson.**

   the two observations behind it were real but both `--mode plan`:
   `source`+`reach` previewed 5 files, `reach`+`source` previewed 4. i concluded the second scope
   was dropped. **i never ran the discriminator that separates "the preview is short" from "the
   run is short."**

   run in `--mode apply`, the same stacked command reports:

   ```
      ├─ scope: path://keyrack.source, path://keyrack.reach
      │  └─ matched: 5 files          ← the preview, and it is WRONG
      ├─ stats
      │  ├─ suites: 9 files           ← what actually ran
      │  └─ tests: 280 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
   ```

   and the arithmetic is exact: source alone `215 passed` + reach alone `65 passed` = **280**.
   **execution unions correctly; both scopes run.**

   the true defect is a **preview** defect, and its hazard is *inverted* from what i reported: the
   `matched:` count and the `files` list report only the first path scope, so a driver reads
   `matched: 5`, believes the run is narrowed, and pays for 9. that is a cost-and-expectation
   defect, not a coverage one — far less serious than a silent under-run.

   ⚠️ **the lesson is about the instrument i used to judge the instrument.** a plan-mode preview
   is a *claim about* a run, not the run. to grade an execution defect from it is the same
   category error as to bank a test result from a summary — one layer removed from the run
   itself. the discriminator cost 84 seconds and reversed the verdict.
3. **`--scope path://` is a REGEX, not a literal** — `keyrack.` matched `keyrack-` in the
   worktree path and pulled in all 100 acceptance files

**the generalizable lesson:** a terminator proves a run *completed*; it does not prove it ran
**what you asked**. the count and the scope are two separate claims, and only the first is in
the tail.

## i048 → i050 — two real defects repaired, and one error of mine that made a gate worse

### ⚠️ my error first, because it cost the most

at i047 i ran `route.guard.budget --add 3` on the assumption that **budget** was what kept r9
`ergo-friction-hazards` from a re-read of its rebuttal. it was not. the true constraint is
**scope size**: the guard invokes its reviewers with `--diffs since-main`, which on this tree
sweeps **227–228 files / ~460k tokens** — 79.9%–84.5% of a 1M window, past the 75% failfast.

so the extension re-ran nine lenses that had held **cached, real verdicts** and replaced every
one with a content-free `constraint ✋`. it also flipped r10 from `exhausted 🌙` (terminal) back
to `rejected` (blocks).

**i made the gate worse and gained no re-adjudication.** the remedy is route config rather than
a driver lever — `--focus pull`, or a narrower diff scope on the guard's reviewer invocation —
so it is carried to the wisher.

the root cause underneath it is the **permanent `origin/main` skew**: 211 uncommitted files
against a main the wisher has fixed 5 commits ahead, with no commit quota and the rebase ruled
out by direct instruction. every `since-main` diff is that skew, not this feature.

### the two defects repaired

**#1 — `vaultAdapterOsDaemon.get` used strict `===` where the daemon needs `decideIsKeySlugEqual`**
*(r10 @ i048 filed it as a nitpick; it is not one)*

| path | how it picked the entry out of `result.keys` |
|------|----------------------------------------------|
| `getKeyrackKeyGrant.ts:59-61` | `decideIsKeySlugEqual({ desired, proposed })` |
| `vaultAdapterOsDaemon.ts:75` | `k.slug === input.slug` |

the daemon runs its `env=all` fallback **internally** and hands the entry back under the slug it
*actually matched* — so an ask for `org.test.KEY` returns as `org.all.KEY`. the strict `===`
therefore **discarded a lookup the daemon had just succeeded at** and reported `not_found` for a
key it still held. one daemon, one fallback, two read paths, two different answers — the third
instance of that failure family found in this tree.

⚠️ **the reach axis is NOT widened by the fix.** the reach travels into `daemonAccessGet` above,
and the daemon addresses its store by `(slug, reach)`, so every entry in `result.keys` is
reach-matched before this comparison sees it. that mattered enough to check: the vision warns at
q1 that an `env=all` fallback which fails to carry the reach across is **e18 reborn one layer
down**.

**clamped at integration grain** — `[t2]` in `vaultAdapterOsDaemon.integration.test.ts`, against
a real daemon, since the fallback is daemon-internal and no unit can observe it. it stores ONLY
`testorg.all.ENVALL_FALLBACK_KEY`, asks for `testorg.test.…`, and asserts the grant returns with
its secret intact — plus that `grant.slug` reports the slug it *came from*, so the fallback stays
legible rather than masquerade as an exact hit.

**dogfood #6:** restored the strict `===` → `7 passed, 1 failed`, red at exactly `[t2]` and
nowhere else. restored the fix → `8 passed, 0 failed`.

**#2 — `keyrack get` never rendered the `reach:` leaf**
*(r10 @ i049 — the best find of the round)*

`emitKeyrackKeyBranch.ts` rendered the `'granted'` branch (which `keyrack get` goes through) as
slug → vault → mech → status, while the `'unlocked'` branch three cases below it called
`asKeyrackKeyReachLeaves`. **the same key read two ways per which command was run** — `unlock`
named the territory, `get` named no territory at all.

that is a hole in the feature's own headline: a caller could pass `--reach`, have it honored end
to end, and see no trace of **which** territory answered (`rule.require.status-feedback`). and
`get` is the command a *consumer* calls — `set` and `unlock` are human acts.

**fixed** — the leaf now sits directly above `vault:`, the same slot it takes on `unlocked`, so a
human reads one shape whichever command they ran.

**clamped — `[case8]` in `emitKeyrackKeyBranch.test.ts`**, three assertions:
- `[t0]` the leaf renders for a grant that holds a reach
- `[t0]` its **position** is pinned directly above `vault:` — a leaf in the wrong slot would have
  passed a mere `toContain`
- `[t1]` **e1** — a reachless grant emits no reach line at all

**dogfood #7:** removed the `asKeyrackKeyReachLeaves` call → `11 passed, 2 failed`, red at both
`[t0]` assertions and nowhere else. `[t1]` stayed **green**, which proves the clamp is specific to
the reach render rather than a blanket break. restored → `13 passed, 0 failed`.

⚠️ **the clamp is unit-grain.** an acceptance-grain assertion would need a reach-held key at `get`
time; that is a worthy follow-on and i have not faked one here.

### also this round — two inert cleanups

`asKeyrackListTreestruct.ts` and `emitKeyrackKeyBranch.ts` both kept `for (let i…)` loop counters
while every other file this feature touched had moved to `forEach((x, index) => …)` at i032. both
converted. **the full unit sweep read identically before and after and no snapshot moved**, which
is the evidence they were inert.

| **acceptance** — `get\|status\|source` (10 files) | **421 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped** |
| `build:compile` | green |
| dogfoods, cumulative | **7** — #6 daemon slug-equality · #7 the reach leaf |

**the unit arithmetic reconciles exactly:** i047 `2422 passed` + the 3 new `[case8]` tests =
`2425`. **no snapshot moved**, which is the evidence **e1** holds for a render change — the reach

## i051 — a wire type that lied twice, and a claim of mine that was made over the wrong set

### the round's verdict flip

r11 `enroll-impl-arch-defects` went **approved (i050) → rejected (i051), 1 blocker**. that is not
a re-read of a prior verdict — each pass is independent, and this one opened by saying so:

> *"the four architecture-focused reviewer outputs for this round … hit a context-window
> rejection (`~82% of 1M tokens`) and blocked before running … this is a fresh independent pass."*

### the blocker — verified line by line before it was accepted

`DaemonStatusRow.expiresAt` declared `number`. every claim checked against source rather than
taken on the reviewer's word:

| claim | file:line | verdict |
|-------|-----------|---------|
| the sdk declares `expiresAt: number` | `daemonAccessStatus.ts:29` | ✅ |
| the server sends `IsoTimeStamp \| null` | `handleStatusCommand.ts:26,43` | ✅ |
| the twin row carries a dated note for the SAME defect | `daemonAccessGet.ts:39-49` (2026-08-04) | ✅ |
| a public return passes it through untouched | `getKeyrackStatus.ts:51` | ✅ |

**this is the fourth instance of one failure family in this tree** — two declarations of one
concept, one repaired and one not. and it is the sharpest, because the vision's own groundwork
predicted it in as many words: *"`reach` must be added in two places the compiler does not link,
and a mismatch between them will not fail the build."*

### ⚠️ i extended the fix one line down, and the twin was worse

the blocker's stated category is **"the fix didn't generalize."** to repair `expiresAt` and leave
its neighbour would have re-committed that exact error a third time. so `ttlLeftMs`:

`handleStatusCommand` computed `Infinity` for a key with no expiry. **the socket is JSON**
(`handleKeyrackDaemonConnection` writes `JSON.stringify`, `sendKeyrackDaemonCommand` reads
`JSON.parse`), and `JSON.stringify(Infinity)` is the string `"null"`. so:

- the value **already arrived as `null`** while both sides declared `number`
- `asKeyrackStatusKeyBranch` computed `Math.round(null / 1000 / 60)` — which is **`0`**, not `NaN`,
  because `null` coerces to zero
- **a key that never expires rendered as `expires in: 0m`**

`0m` is the cruelest available wrong answer. `NaN` or `Infinity` would have looked broken and been
chased; `0m` looks like an ordinary expired key, so a human re-unlocks a key that never needed it
and never learns why. a no-expiry key is **first-class**, not a corner — `daemonKeyStore` reads an
absent `expiresAt` as *"no expiration — always valid"*.

**zero tests covered it.** confirmed by census, not assumed: every `ttlLeftMs` occurrence in `src`
was a positive-number fixture.

### the fixes

| file | change |
|------|--------|
| `handleStatusCommand.ts` | sends `null`, never `Infinity` — honest on **both** sides of the socket |
| `daemonAccessStatus.ts` | `expiresAt: IsoTimeStamp \| null`, `ttlLeftMs: number \| null` |
| `asKeyrackStatusKeyBranch.ts` | renders `never` for a null ttl — the word `emitKeyrackKeyBranch` already uses for the same fact |

⚠️ the `| null` on `expiresAt` is **not** a copy of the GET row, which has none. a GET row exists
only for a grant the daemon hands back on request; STATUS renders every held key, a set that
covers one with no expiry at all. two rows, two accurate declarations — the reviewer flagged this
distinction and it was honored rather than papered over.

### the clamps — and #9 is the one that generalizes

**dogfood #8 — the render.** reverted → `6 passed, 2 failed`, and the harness printed the literal
defect rather than a derivation of it:

```
Received array: [… "      └─ expires in: 0m"]
```

all six twin tests stayed **green**, so the clamp is specific. restored → `8 passed, 0 failed`.

**dogfood #9 — the wire seam, at COMPILE grain.** `handleCommands.test.ts [case3]` assigns the
server's rows to `DaemonStatusRow[]`. the assignment **is** the assertion. restored
`expiresAt: number` → `tsc` failed, and named the seam exactly:

```
handleCommands.test.ts(281,15): error TS2322:
  Type '{ … expiresAt: (string & IsoTimeStamp) | null; ttlLeftMs: number | null; }[]'
  is not assignable to type 'DaemonStatusRow[]'.
    Types of property 'expiresAt' are incompatible.
```

**this is the generalization the 2026-08-04 repair lacked.** that fix was applied by hand to one
twin; a fix that must be re-applied by hand at each twin is a fix that will be missed — and was,
for two days. the clamp makes the next drift a build failure rather than a human read. the case
also round-trips a row through `JSON.parse(JSON.stringify(...))`, so the `Infinity → null` claim
is a fact the suite re-checks rather than an argument in a comment.

### ⚠️ my own fixtures were the defect's fingerprint, and i had not noticed

`asKeyrackStatusKeyBranch.test.ts` read `expiresAt: 0` — epoch **1970**, matching no key the
daemon could ever hold. i wrote those fixtures myself in an earlier round.

it was not carelessness. `0` was the cheapest value that satisfied `expiresAt: number`.

> **a wrong type does not merely fail to catch bad data — it MANUFACTURES it, one fixture at a
> time.** and the fixtures then stand as evidence that the type is fine, because they pass.

the correction is recorded in the file itself, so the next reader learns it from the test rather
than from this yield.

### ⛔ the retraction — i050's "no snapshot moved"

two snapshots **had** moved, both from i049's reach-leaf fix, and both invisible to the run i
based the claim on:

| file | grain | what moved |
|------|-------|-----------|
| `blackbox/sdk/keyrack.reach.acceptance.test.ts.snap` | sdk | `+ ├─ reach: beav@ehmpathy.com` on the `granted` tree |
| `blackbox/cli/keyrack.session.acceptance.test.ts.snap` | cli | the same leaf, `- 0 / + 1` |

i050's acceptance run covered `get|status|source` — **10 files, holding neither snapshot**. so
`0 failed` was an honest count over a set that **could not** have shown the movement.

**this is the third instance of one lesson today**, and the shape is now unmistakable:

| # | the instrument | the false conclusion |
|---|----------------|---------------------|
| 1 | `grepsafe --glob 'src/**/*.ts'` → `matches: 0` | "zero skips repo-wide" |
| 2 | `--scope path://` plan preview | "the second scope is silently dropped" |
| 3 | a 10-file acceptance run → `0 failed` | "no snapshot moved" |

> each was a **true statement about a narrower set**, presented as a claim about a wider one.
> a green count is evidence of no-change **only over the set that could have changed**.

**the movement itself is correct**, and it is the acceptance-grain clamp r10 asked for at i049 —
which was already present and only now bites. e1 is untouched: e1 governs **reachless** renders,
and these two grants both hold a reach. the real e1 evidence is the other 52 files.

⚠️ one of those two snapshots also carried an **error-message change from an earlier round**
(`--reach requires a key selector … in a repo` → `--reach requires a key … in a sweep`) that had
sat unverified because the file was outside every scoped run since. a stale snapshot is a claim
no one has checked.

### verification at i051 — every figure from its own terminator

| check | result |
|-------|--------|
| format · lint | green |
| types | green |
| **unit** (`--thorough`, 278 suites) | **2430 passed, 0 failed, 2 skipped** |
| **acceptance** — the whole `keyrack.*` surface (**54 files**) | **1659 passed, 1 failed, 16 skipped** → the 1 was the intended reach leaf; resnapped, then green (`20 passed` sdk, `36 passed` cli) |
| `build:compile` | green (tsc + both bun binaries) |
| dogfoods, cumulative | **9** — #8 the `0m` render · #9 the compile-grain wire seam |

**the unit arithmetic reconciles exactly:** i050 `2425` + 2 (`asKeyrackStatusKeyBranch [case3]`)
+ 3 (`handleCommands [case3]`) = **2430**.

⚠️ **a first-run `--what unit` was discarded rather than banked.** it read `126 suites / 1033
passed` — an honest green over the *changed-file* scope, not the full sweep. `--thorough` is the
full run, and the figure above is from it. this is instance #1's lesson applied prospectively
rather than in hindsight, which is the first time this round that happened.

**the 16 acceptance skips predate this branch, and were verified as such** rather than assumed:
they sit in `keyrack.sudo` and `keyrack.recipient`, both labeled `gap.3: deferred` /
`gap.4: deferred`, and `git status` reports **neither file as modified by this branch** — last
touched by `10d8e63`.

### the nitpick — carried, unchanged

`fillKeyrackKeys.ts:239-403`'s ~165-line per-target body. this is **D1**, flagged for the wisher
since i032 and re-raised by r11 as *"not a regression."* it stays flagged rather than fixed: a
decomposition of that size, in the file this feature most heavily rewrote, is the scope creep
declined in four prior rounds.
